# RFC 4301 - Security Architecture for the Internet Protocol

IPsec architecture. Every requirement listed for RFC 4301, the tests linked to it, and their recorded reviews. This summary is included in the automatic compliance check, ./le rfc check.

## Overview

### Positive

what Ze has

| Measure | Value | Count | What it means |
|---|---:|---|---|
| Tested both ways | 55 | of 101 mandatory requirements | a positive test checks that Ze does what the requirement demands and a negative one checks that it refuses what the requirement forbids |
| One polarity plus reason | 12 | of 101 mandatory requirements | the requirement admits no counter-case, so one polarity plus a recorded reason supplies the complete test coverage required |
| One polarity, unexcused | 0 | of 101 mandatory requirements | one direction is tested, the other is neither tested nor excused, and nothing states which |
| Tests shown to detect a fault | 172 | 172 of 242 tests linked to requirements, 9 without a fault test and 45 with outdated fault tests | each test was observed to fail when the code it checks was deliberately broken. The test, its stated claim, and the relevant code are checked against the saved record for changes; the deliberate fault is not run again. Having both positive and negative tests verifies a requirement only after both tests have been shown to detect a fault |

### Negative

what Ze owes

| Measure | Value | Count | What it means |
|---|---:|---|---|
| Partial test coverage | 6 | of 101 mandatory requirements | scoped tests exist; the remaining obligation is unmet or unverified, with zero whole-requirement credit |
| Declared gap or no test | 25 | of 101 mandatory requirements | the requirements the summary declares a gap for, which say what Ze does not do yet, plus the ones no test carries and no annotation excuses. The at-a-glance table lists them apart: declared gaps (at every level), and mandatory requirements with no test and no annotation |
| Audit verdicts | 52 | of 101 mandatory requirements reviewed | 9 weak, wrong or unimplemented, 26 no longer current. Each is named below under its own requirement id |

### Neutral

measures that are neither good news nor bad

| Measure | Value | Count | What it means |
|---|---:|---|---|
| Out of scope | 0 | of 101 mandatory requirements | an obligation that does not bind Ze, counted once its evidence passes. A {not-applicable} annotation says it never bound; a {feature-declined} annotation says its condition is an optional feature Ze does not offer, and quotes the RFC sentence that makes it optional. An annotation whose evidence fails is not counted here: the figures count it as exclusion unchecked. This classification records applicability and forms part of the total accounted for by the cards on this page |
| Not applicable | 0 | of 101 mandatory requirements | a {not-applicable} annotation says the obligation does not bind Ze. No test is owed only once its evidence passes; one whose evidence fails stays owed, and the figures count it as exclusion unchecked. It remains in the total that these cards add up to |
| Met below Ze | 3 | of 101 mandatory requirements | a {lower-layer} annotation says a layer under Ze performs the behavior, on state Ze installs into that layer, and names the producer that installs it. The obligation binds Ze and stays in the MUST bar, outside Ze verified: it is OS verified once a tagged test checks that layer's behavior on the state Ze installs, with a recorded break, and an OS testing gap until then |
| Optional feature declined | 0 | of 101 mandatory requirements | a {feature-declined} annotation says the obligation is conditional on a feature the RFC makes optional and Ze does not offer, and it quotes the sentence that makes it optional. The condition is false, so nothing is owed once that quote passes its evidence check; one whose quote fails stays owed, and the figures count it as exclusion unchecked. It remains in the total that these cards add up to |
| Linux cannot do this | 0 | of 101 mandatory requirements | Linux cannot provide the primitive this requirement needs. The obligation still binds Ze and stays unmet, but as a limit of the kernel, not of Ze's code: once its limit entry is verified, the row is not owed (OS blocked) and is left out of the MUST bar; one whose entry fails stays owed, and the figures count it as not built. It remains in the total that these cards add up to and earns no test verification credit |

### Totals

what the cards above are counted against

| Measure | Value | Count | What it means |
|---|---:|---|---|
| Mandatory requirements (MUST level) | 101 | of 104 requirements this summary lists, at all levels | The RFC's MUST, MUST NOT, SHALL, SHALL NOT and REQUIRED requirements included in the automatic checks: the compliance check (./le rfc check) holds Ze to each of them. This is the total being assessed; the counts above show the recorded status of those requirements. |

The 9 counts marked as a part above are the whole of the 101 mandatory requirements: they add up to 101. Tests shown to detect a fault counts individual tests rather than requirements, so its count is separate.

A color names what the measure MEANS, not how well Ze scores on it. Green is a good outcome at any value, red is a bad one, and neither a population nor a scope count is an outcome, so both take no color. The number under the label is what says how far Ze has got. An outlined card with no colored edge, under Totals, is a total the other cards are counted against, not a measure.

| Card | Tone here | Why that color |
|---|---|---|
| Out of scope | neutral | no color: an obligation that never bound Ze is neither an achievement nor a failure, and counting it either way would be a claim |
| Tested both ways | ok | green at every value: a test pair is the outcome this gate exists to produce, and the count under the label is what says how far Ze has got |
| One polarity plus reason | ok | green at every value: where no counter-case exists, one polarity IS the complete answer, and a recorded reason is what the gate demands beside it |
| One polarity, unexcused | ok | green at zero, RED above it: one test direction is missing without a recorded reason |
| Partial test coverage | bad | green at zero, RED above it: testing one clause does not verify the whole requirement |
| Declared gap or no test | bad | green at zero, RED above it: a binding obligation that nothing shows Ze meets is owed, whether or not a reason is stated |
| Not applicable | neutral | no color: an obligation that never bound Ze is neither an achievement nor a failure, and counting it either way would be a claim |
| Met below Ze | neutral | no color: an obligation met below Ze is neither a test Ze wrote nor work Ze owes, and the two green counts above are what says how much Ze's own tests verify |
| Optional feature declined | neutral | no color: an obligation whose condition Ze never meets is neither an achievement nor a failure. The absent FEATURE is disclosed on the RFC's own status row, as an implementation gap a later scope decision can revisit |
| Linux cannot do this | neutral | neutral at every value: an evidenced platform limit is neither test verification nor a scope exemption |
| Tests shown to detect a fault | ok | green at every value: these tests have been shown to detect a deliberate fault. This card counts individual tests separately, so its total cannot be added to the requirement counts above |
| Audit verdicts | bad | RED on the first weak, wrong or unimplemented verdict, amber while a verdict is no longer current or a mandatory requirement is unreviewed, green when every one is judged sound and current |
| Mandatory requirements (MUST level) | outlined | outlined, with no colored edge: it is the total the other cards are counted against, not a measure, and a larger one is neither good news nor bad |

## Automatic checks

Included in automatic checks: Security Architecture for IP (RFC 4301): native control-plane SPD/SAD projected to kernel XFRM. The extraction walk of 2026-09-21 read all 89 normative sites in `rfc/full/rfc4301.txt` and grew the checklist from 24 rows to 81 (72 MUST, 6 MUST NOT, 2 SHOULD, 1 MAY). 14 rows carry an annotation from the 2026-08-30 pass: 1 gap (port/ICMP selectors), 7 single-polarity positive (tunnel/transport modes, SPD present, negotiated selectors, manual+automated keying), 6 not-applicable (per-packet datapath kernel-delegated). The 57 rows added on 2026-09-21 carried no annotation and no test when added; the ruling pass of the same day classified every one, each on its own row.

The MUST bar shows mandatory requirements (MUST, MUST NOT, REQUIRED, SHALL and SHALL NOT) that Ze and its operating system must meet. It includes only RFCs whose requirement lists have been checked against the original text. Ze verified means a test has been shown to fail when the required behavior is deliberately broken. Behavior provided by the operating system or a library is OS verified when a test checks it using the settings Ze installs and has also been shown to detect a deliberate fault; until then, it is an OS testing gap. Tests awaiting verification counts requirements a tagged test covers that no deliberate fault has yet been shown to break and that nothing records as unmet. Exclusion unchecked counts requirements claimed not to apply to Ze whose evidence is still to be checked. Not assessed counts requirements no tagged test covers and that nothing records as unmet, so nothing yet says whether Ze meets them. Not built counts requirements the RFC summary or a test review says Ze does not meet, in full or in part. The overview bar adds optional requirements (MAY, NOT RECOMMENDED, OPTIONAL, RECOMMENDED, SHOULD and SHOULD NOT) and requirements that do not apply to Ze. An RFC marked requirements not reviewed is excluded from these figures because its requirement list has not been checked against the original text. Counts are loaded from the RFC data files.

Source: [data/rfc/rfc4301.json](../../../data/rfc/rfc4301.json).

## Requirements

| Requirement | Text | Level | Section | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RFC4301-4.1-1` | a) A host implementation of IPsec MUST support both transport and tunnel mode. (§4.1) | MUST | 4.1 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestChildSAInstallsInDataplane`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/engine/child_test.go#L177). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestIPsecSAPerDestinationWithOSPFSelector`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/plugins/ospf/ipsec_install_test.go#L246). **negative:** no negative test. **{single-polarity}:** ze projects both modes -- IKE child SAs install tunnel-mode ESP and OSPFv3 RFC 4552 installs transport-mode ESP/AH; a capability-presence MUST has no meaningful negative (internal/component/ike/engine/child.go:224, :253, internal/plugins/ospf/ipsec_install.go:413, :441) |
| `RFC4301-4.1-2` | A security gateway MUST support tunnel mode (§4.1) | MUST | 4.1 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestChildSAInstallsInDataplane`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/engine/child_test.go#L192). **negative:** no negative test. **{single-polarity}:** ze (a security gateway) installs tunnel-mode ESP for every IKE-negotiated peer SA and policy (internal/component/ike/engine/child.go:224, :253, :281, :295) |
| `RFC4301-4.1-3` | Aside from the two exceptions below, whenever either end of a security association is a security gateway, the SA MUST be tunnel mode. (§4.1) | MUST | 4.1 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestChildSAInstallsInDataplane`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/engine/child_test.go#L179). **negative:** no negative test. **{single-polarity}:** the IKE child-SA path hardcodes tunnel mode for every peer SA, so a peer SA can never be transport (internal/component/ike/engine/child.go:40, :224, :253) |
| `RFC4301-4.1-4` | IKE creates pairs of SAs, so for simplicity, we choose to require that both SAs in a pair be of the same mode, transport or tunnel. (§4.1) | MUST | 4.1 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestChildSAInstallsInDataplane`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/engine/child_test.go#L181). **negative:** no negative test. **{single-polarity}:** the inbound and outbound child SAs of a pair are both built with modeTunnel, so the pair is always same-mode (internal/component/ike/engine/child.go:224, :253) |
| `RFC4301-4.1-5` | To permit this, the IPsec implementation MUST permit establishment and maintenance of multiple SAs between a given sender and receiver, with the same selectors. (§4.1) | MUST | 4.1 | **positive:** no positive test. **negative:** no negative test. **{gap}:** two halves. On Linux, XFRM (linux:CONFIG_XFRM_USER) keys the SAD by SPI, so SAs with identical selectors can coexist, and internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::InstallSA installs each by its SPI, but no test shows ze's IKE keeps two Child SAs with the same selectors to one peer installed together; on the VPP backend (internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp.go::InstallSA) the behaviour is unverified |
| `RFC4301-4.2-1` | Note: A compliant implementation MUST NOT allow instantiation of an ESP SA that employs both NULL encryption and no integrity algorithm. (§4.2) | MUST NOT | 4.2 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestIPsecESPRequiresIntegrity`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/plugins/ospf/config_ipsec_test.go#L121). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestRFC4301ESPNullEncryptionWithIntegrityIsInstantiated`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_esp_null_linux_test.go#L17). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestIPsecESPRequiresIntegrity`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/plugins/ospf/config_ipsec_test.go#L102). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestRFC4301ESPNullEncryptionWithoutIntegrityIsRefused`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_esp_null_linux_test.go#L36). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestRFC4301VPPESPNullEncryptionWithoutIntegrityIsRefused`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_esp_null_vpp_test.go#L29) |
| `RFC4301-4.4.1-1` | An IPsec implementation MUST have at least one SPD, and it MAY support multiple SPDs, if appropriate for the context in which the IPsec implementation operates. (§4.4.1) | MUST | 4.4.1 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestChildSAInstallsInDataplane`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/engine/child_test.go#L158). **negative:** no negative test. **{single-polarity}:** ze maintains an SPD policy model (SPParams) and installs at least the PROTECT entries into the kernel SPD via both the IKE and OSPFv3 paths (internal/component/ike/dataplane/dataplane.go:145, engine/child.go:276, :290, internal/plugins/ospf/ipsec_install.go:432-452) |
| `RFC4301-4.4.1-2` | The SPD, or relevant caches, must be consulted during the processing of all traffic (inbound and outbound), including traffic not protected by IPsec, that traverses the IPsec boundary. | `RFC4301-4.4.1.1-1` | The following selector parameters MUST be supported by all IPsec implementations to facilitate control of SA granularity. (§4.4.1.1) | MUST | 4.4.1.1 | **positive:** no positive test. **negative:** no negative test. **{gap}:** Ze's selectors carry IPv4 and IPv6 prefixes, a next-layer protocol, ANY or one port per side, and an ICMP type and code (dataplane.SPParams UpperProto, SrcPort and DstPort; an operator SPD entry takes one type and a code range, ipsec/spd_policy.go::parseICMPSelector). Missing: an address range that is not a prefix in the configuration (each entry takes one CIDR prefix; only a received selector is tiled into prefixes, engine/ts_narrow.go::rangePrefixes); a port range (ipsec/spd_policy.go::parseSPDPort and ipsec/traffic_selector.go::parsePortSelector accept ANY or one port); OPAQUE ports and the IPv6 OPAQUE next-layer protocol (RFC4301-4.4.1.1-2); exact Mobility Header type selectors: ipsec/traffic_selector.go::ValidForProtocol preserves the RFC-valid high-octet value, but ProgrammableForProtocol refuses constrained MH because Linux 7.3 net/xfrm/xfrm_policy.c::decode_session6 does not fill the received Type read by include/net/xfrm.h::xfrm_flowi_sport. The actual Child/backend guest probe delivered ANY Types 5/7 on local IN and routed FWD, while an exact Type 5 policy dropped matching Type 5 on both paths. Config, negotiation and Child installation now refuse exact MH before state changes; ANY remains usable. The wanted receive-flow extraction feature is plan/post-release/kernel/spec-xfrm-ipv6-mh-selector.md; and the Name selector (RFC4301-4.4.1.1-5) |

| `RFC4301-5.2-1` | Since the SPD-I is just a part of the SPD, if a packet that is looked up in the SPD-I cannot be matched to an entry there, then the packet MUST be discarded. (§4.4.1) | MUST | 4.4.1 | **positive:** no positive test. **negative:** no negative test. **{gap}:** re-examined under RULINGS R28 and narrowed by the owner decision of 2026-10-20. Where the catch-all is installed (installUnmatched, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched.go, ranked last at dataplane.PriorityUnmatched) every inbound packet meets an SPD-I entry and the action is the RFC4301-4.4.1-3 choice. Two paths remain where an inbound packet can meet no SPD-I entry and the dataplane delivers it. The VPP backend is the first: it holds no entry bound to no interface (vppPolicyInterface; vppBackend.CatchAllSupported answers dataplane.ErrNoNodeWideSPD, internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp_policy.go), so it runs only under `unmatched bypass` (the default discard is refused at verify there) and installs no catch-all. OSPFv3 authentication with RFC 4552 manual keys is the second: the OSPF plugin installs its SAs and transport-mode SPD entries through whichever dataplane backend is active, loading the xfrm backend itself only when none is (defaultDataplaneSource, internal/plugins/ospf/ipsec_install.go), and the IKE engine neither installs nor releases those. On XFRM, while the IKE engine holds an applied configuration under `unmatched discard`, its if_id 0 catch-all (installUnmatched, ranked last) sits in the same SPD below them and is their final entry, read from the code and not yet tested; with no `vpn ipsec` block, after the engine's releaseSPD, under `unmatched bypass`, and on VPP, they are in force with no final DISCARD entry (owned by plan/post-release/features/spec-ipsec-spd-final-discard-vpp-ospf.md); a packet they do not match meets no SPD-I entry and the kernel delivers it. Whether that path gains a catch-all is an owner scope decision not yet taken. Everywhere else a catch-all that cannot be installed fails the apply under either disposition, a platform without XFRM and a missing dataplane included (TestUnmatchedInstallFailureFailsTheApply, TestUnmatchedWithNoEnforcingDataplaneIsAnError), and so does the final entry of each bound XFRM interface's own SPD at its if_id, whose refused install also keeps that interface's if_id 0 exemptions out (installXFRMInterfaceEntries, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched_xfrm_interface.go; TestInterfaceSPDCatchAllInstallFailureFailsTheApply), and `unmatched discard` is refused at verify where the dataplane says ahead of time it cannot hold the entry (verifyUnmatchedEnforceable). This window statement is about the IKE engine's SPD alone, and the OSPFv3 entries above are outside it. Outside the window from the first configuration apply to the IKE engine's exit, the IKE engine holds no SPD entry and no SA: applyConfig (internal/component/ike/engine/apply.go) installs the catch-all first and the IKE bypass after it, and on every exit shutdown stops each peer (removing its Child SA), closes the IKE sockets, then releaseSPD removes the catch-all last, and keeps it installed with a warning when any other removal failed (warnSPDLeft, spd_left.go). So in those windows the IKE engine is not acting as an IPsec implementation and no packet is looked up in an SPD-I of its own, a reading the owner accepted on 2026-10-20 (TestRunInstallsNoSPDEntryBeforeTheFirstApply, TestRFC4301FinalEntryIsUnderEveryZeSPDEntry, TestReleaseSPDKeepsTheCatchAllWhenARemovalFails). TestRFC4301InboundClearPacketMatchingNoEntryFollowsTheUnmatchedLeaf shows the installed catch-all deciding an inbound clear packet on XFRM |

Tested: "match the packet against the inbound selectors identified by the SAD entry". Gap: "verify that the received packet is appropriate for the SA via which it was received". These locate one sentence; the split remains by tested case, not independent matching and verification obligations. All nine tagged covers across six bodies were reread. These normal observed fixtures resolve that implementation/test precondition; they do not add tags or claims to these nine covers, establish general RFC kernel-discrimination or authorize private fault preparation. Each cover carries a discrimination record (xfrmPolicyAction BLOCK->ALLOW, parseUnmatched disabled, shutdown releaseSPD-before-stop, planXFRMInterfaceEntries catchAll[:0], interfaceSPDCatchAll DISCARD->BYPASS, installXFRMInterfaceEntries noFinalEntry dropped, releaseSPD warnSPDLeft && false); none breaks applyConfig, so the edit stales none, and the releaseSPD record the previous note called owed is now present. | `RFC4301-4.4.1-13` | Note that when an SPD entry is decorrelated all the resulting entries MUST be linked together, so that all members of the group derived from an individual, SPD entry (prior to decorrelation) can all be placed into caches and into the SAD at the same time. The obligation stays declared until a scope decision of Thomas's declines decorrelation or ze decorrelates its SPD |

mapped"; gap "to an appropriate value for the domain"; dscp-map copies the inner DSCP or fixes one outer DSCP per peer, and the tags prove that treatment reaches the SA on VPP for any value and on Linux XFRM for copy and 0, and that a value a dataplane cannot write is refused at commit and again at install rather than installed as another; Gap is an appropriate value Ze cannot write: a fixed value 1 to 63 on Linux XFRM, which internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmDSCPFlag refuses because the XFRM state writes only the inner DSCP or 0 (LL-XFRM-OUTER-DF-SET-DSCP, docs/architecture/linux-limitations.md, a design constraint and not a kernel limit: an nftables rewrite of the outer header could write it), an owner-decided refusal never counted conformant whose Ze kernel patch is planned after the first release (plan/post-release/kernel/spec-xfrm-outer-df-set-dscp.md), and a per-class mapping table, which is not built; plan/release/fixes/spec-rfc4301-architecture-gaps.md. **Partial test coverage:** Tests and fault test records cover only the part marked Tested; they do not verify the whole requirement. not reviewed: no reviewer has checked whether these tests enforce the requirement |

| `RFC4301-5.2-9` | (No SAs are created in response to receipt of a packet that requires IPsec protection; only BYPASS or DISCARD cache entries can be created this way.) If there is no match, discard the traffic. (§5.2) | MUST | 5.2 | **positive:** no positive test. **negative:** no negative test. **{gap}:** re-examined under RULINGS R28 and narrowed by the owner decision of 2026-10-20. Where the catch-all is installed (installUnmatched, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched.go, ranked last at dataplane.PriorityUnmatched) every inbound packet meets an SPD-I entry and the action is the RFC4301-4.4.1-3 choice. Two paths remain where an inbound packet can meet no SPD-I entry and the dataplane delivers it. The VPP backend is the first: it holds no entry bound to no interface (vppPolicyInterface; vppBackend.CatchAllSupported answers dataplane.ErrNoNodeWideSPD, internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp_policy.go), so it runs only under `unmatched bypass` (the default discard is refused at verify there) and installs no catch-all. OSPFv3 authentication with RFC 4552 manual keys is the second: the OSPF plugin installs its SAs and transport-mode SPD entries through whichever dataplane backend is active, loading the xfrm backend itself only when none is (defaultDataplaneSource, internal/plugins/ospf/ipsec_install.go), and the IKE engine neither installs nor releases those. On XFRM, while the IKE engine holds an applied configuration under `unmatched discard`, its if_id 0 catch-all (installUnmatched, ranked last) sits in the same SPD below them and is their final entry, read from the code and not yet tested; with no `vpn ipsec` block, after the engine's releaseSPD, under `unmatched bypass`, and on VPP, they are in force with no final DISCARD entry (owned by plan/post-release/features/spec-ipsec-spd-final-discard-vpp-ospf.md); a packet they do not match meets no SPD-I entry and the kernel delivers it. Whether that path gains a catch-all is an owner scope decision not yet taken. Everywhere else a catch-all that cannot be installed fails the apply under either disposition, a platform without XFRM and a missing dataplane included (TestUnmatchedInstallFailureFailsTheApply, TestUnmatchedWithNoEnforcingDataplaneIsAnError), and so does the final entry of each bound XFRM interface's own SPD at its if_id, whose refused install also keeps that interface's if_id 0 exemptions out (installXFRMInterfaceEntries, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched_xfrm_interface.go; TestInterfaceSPDCatchAllInstallFailureFailsTheApply), and `unmatched discard` is refused at verify where the dataplane says ahead of time it cannot hold the entry (verifyUnmatchedEnforceable). This window statement is about the IKE engine's SPD alone, and the OSPFv3 entries above are outside it. Outside the window from the first configuration apply to the IKE engine's exit, the IKE engine holds no SPD entry and no SA: applyConfig (internal/component/ike/engine/apply.go) installs the catch-all first and the IKE bypass after it, and on every exit shutdown stops each peer (removing its Child SA), closes the IKE sockets, then releaseSPD removes the catch-all last, and keeps it installed with a warning when any other removal failed (warnSPDLeft, spd_left.go). So in those windows the IKE engine is not acting as an IPsec implementation and no packet is looked up in an SPD-I of its own, a reading the owner accepted on 2026-10-20 (TestRunInstallsNoSPDEntryBeforeTheFirstApply, TestRFC4301FinalEntryIsUnderEveryZeSPDEntry, TestReleaseSPDKeepsTheCatchAllWhenARemovalFails). TestRFC4301InboundClearPacketMatchingNoEntryFollowsTheUnmatchedLeaf shows the installed catch-all deciding an inbound clear packet on XFRM |

| `RFC4301-8.2.1-1` | When such traffic arrives, if the traffic would exceed the updated PMTU value the traffic MUST be handled as follows: Case 1: Original (cleartext) packet is IPv4 and has the DF bit set. The implementation SHOULD discard the packet and send a PMTU ICMP message. Case 2: Original (cleartext) packet is IPv4 and has the DF bit clear. The implementation SHOULD fragment (before or after encryption per its configuration) and then forward the fragments. It SHOULD NOT send a PMTU ICMP message. Case 3: Original (cleartext) packet is IPv6. The implementation SHOULD discard the packet and send a PMTU ICMP message. (§8.2.1) | MUST | 8.2.1 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMPolicyPMTUDiscardsAndSignalsOverAnIPv6OuterSA`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_pmtu_integration_linux_test.go#L170). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMPolicyPMTUDiscardsAndSignalsOverTheSAPMTU`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_pmtu_integration_linux_test.go#L159). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMTunnelPMTUDiscardsAndSignalsOverAnIPv6OuterSA`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_pmtu_integration_linux_test.go#L146). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMTunnelPMTUDiscardsAndSignalsOverTheSAPMTU`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_pmtu_integration_linux_test.go#L133). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMPolicyPMTUFragmentsADFClearPacket`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_pmtu_integration_linux_test.go#L238). **negative:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMTunnelPMTUFragmentsADFClearPacket`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_pmtu_integration_linux_test.go#L227) |
| `RFC4301-8.2.2-1` | In all IPsec implementations, the PMTU associated with an SA MUST be "aged" and some mechanism is required to update the PMTU in a timely manner (§8.2.2) | MUST | 8.2.2 | **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMPolicyPMTUAgesBackToTheLinkMTU`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_pmtu_integration_linux_test.go#L291). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMTunnelPMTUAgesBackToTheLinkMTU`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_pmtu_integration_linux_test.go#L280). **positive:** `unit/verify` [`TestXFRMTunnelPMTUAgesBackToTheLinkMTUOverIPv6`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_pmtu_ipv6_integration_linux_test.go#L34). **negative:** no negative test. **{lower-layer}:** Linux route PMTU exceptions aged by net.ipv4.route.mtu_expires and net.ipv6.route.mtu_expires (__ip_rt_update_pmtu in net/ipv4/route.c, rt6_do_update_pmtu in net/ipv6/route.c) under the XFRM bundle that derives the SA inner MTU (xfrm_bundle_ok in net/xfrm/xfrm_policy.c), linux:CONFIG_XFRM_USER; under the default df-bit copy, internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmNoPMTUDisc leaves the outer DF bit copied on the state Ze installs, so a router's Fragmentation Needed or Packet Too Big about the SA's ESP packets lowers the outer path PMTU and the kernel ages it; under df-bit clear the state carries XFRM_STATE_NOPMTUDISC (which the kernel also sets on an IPv4 state not flagged inbound whenever net.ipv4.ip_no_pmtu_disc is set, __xfrm_init_state in net/xfrm/xfrm_state.c), xfrm4_tunnel_encap_add writes the IPv4 outer DF clear, routers fragment the ESP packets and return nothing, so no PMTU is learned from them, and the SA's PMTU is the outer route's MTU, the first-hop link's or one other traffic to the same endpoint learned, aged the same way; the period is the operator's `sysctl setting net.ipv4.route.mtu_expires` or `net.ipv6.route.mtu_expires` |

## Gaps and untested MUSTs

| Requirement | State | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| [`RFC4301-4.1-5`](#rfc4301-4.1-5) To permit this, the IPsec implementation MUST permit establishment and maintenance of multiple SAs between a given sender and receiver, with the same selectors. (§4.1) | {gap}, no test | two halves. On Linux, XFRM (linux:CONFIG_XFRM_USER) keys the SAD by SPI, so SAs with identical selectors can coexist, and internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::InstallSA installs each by its SPI, but no test shows ze's IKE keeps two Child SAs with the same selectors to one peer installed together; on the VPP backend (internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp.go::InstallSA) the behaviour is unverified |
| [`RFC4301-4.4.1-2`](#rfc4301-4.4.1-2) The SPD, or relevant caches, must be consulted during the processing of all traffic (inbound and outbound), including traffic not protected by IPsec, that traverses the IPsec boundary. This includes IPsec management traffic such as IKE. (§4.4.1) | {gap}, no test | two halves. On Linux, XFRM consults the SPD ze installs (internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::InstallPolicy, including the forward policy RequiresForwardPolicy asks for) on every packet crossing the boundary, but no test reads the installed policies for traffic ze does not protect, IKE included; on the VPP backend (internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp_policy.go) SPD consultation of all traffic is unverified |
| [`RFC4301-4.4.1.1-1`](#rfc4301-4.4.1.1-1) The following selector parameters MUST be supported by all IPsec implementations to facilitate control of SA granularity. (§4.4.1.1) | {gap}, no test | Ze's selectors carry IPv4 and IPv6 prefixes, a next-layer protocol, ANY or one port per side, and an ICMP type and code (dataplane.SPParams UpperProto, SrcPort and DstPort; an operator SPD entry takes one type and a code range, ipsec/spd_policy.go::parseICMPSelector). Missing: an address range that is not a prefix in the configuration (each entry takes one CIDR prefix; only a received selector is tiled into prefixes, engine/ts_narrow.go::rangePrefixes); a port range (ipsec/spd_policy.go::parseSPDPort and ipsec/traffic_selector.go::parsePortSelector accept ANY or one port); OPAQUE ports and the IPv6 OPAQUE next-layer protocol (RFC4301-4.4.1.1-2); exact Mobility Header type selectors: ipsec/traffic_selector.go::ValidForProtocol preserves the RFC-valid high-octet value, but ProgrammableForProtocol refuses constrained MH because Linux 7.3 net/xfrm/xfrm_policy.c::decode_session6 does not fill the received Type read by include/net/xfrm.h::xfrm_flowi_sport. The actual Child/backend guest probe delivered ANY Types 5/7 on local IN and routed FWD, while an exact Type 5 policy dropped matching Type 5 on both paths. Config, negotiation and Child installation now refuse exact MH before state changes; ANY remains usable. The wanted receive-flow extraction feature is plan/post-release/kernel/spec-xfrm-ipv6-mh-selector.md; and the Name selector (RFC4301-4.4.1.1-5) |
| [`RFC4301-5.2-1`](#rfc4301-5.2-1) Since the SPD-I is just a part of the SPD, if a packet that is looked up in the SPD-I cannot be matched to an entry there, then the packet MUST be discarded. (§4.4.1) | {gap}, no test | re-examined under RULINGS R28 and narrowed by the owner decision of 2026-10-20. Where the catch-all is installed (installUnmatched, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched.go, ranked last at dataplane.PriorityUnmatched) every inbound packet meets an SPD-I entry and the action is the RFC4301-4.4.1-3 choice. Two paths remain where an inbound packet can meet no SPD-I entry and the dataplane delivers it. The VPP backend is the first: it holds no entry bound to no interface (vppPolicyInterface; vppBackend.CatchAllSupported answers dataplane.ErrNoNodeWideSPD, internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp_policy.go), so it runs only under `unmatched bypass` (the default discard is refused at verify there) and installs no catch-all. OSPFv3 authentication with RFC 4552 manual keys is the second: the OSPF plugin installs its SAs and transport-mode SPD entries through whichever dataplane backend is active, loading the xfrm backend itself only when none is (defaultDataplaneSource, internal/plugins/ospf/ipsec_install.go), and the IKE engine neither installs nor releases those. On XFRM, while the IKE engine holds an applied configuration under `unmatched discard`, its if_id 0 catch-all (installUnmatched, ranked last) sits in the same SPD below them and is their final entry, read from the code and not yet tested; with no `vpn ipsec` block, after the engine's releaseSPD, under `unmatched bypass`, and on VPP, they are in force with no final DISCARD entry (owned by plan/post-release/features/spec-ipsec-spd-final-discard-vpp-ospf.md); a packet they do not match meets no SPD-I entry and the kernel delivers it. Whether that path gains a catch-all is an owner scope decision not yet taken. Everywhere else a catch-all that cannot be installed fails the apply under either disposition, a platform without XFRM and a missing dataplane included (TestUnmatchedInstallFailureFailsTheApply, TestUnmatchedWithNoEnforcingDataplaneIsAnError), and so does the final entry of each bound XFRM interface's own SPD at its if_id, whose refused install also keeps that interface's if_id 0 exemptions out (installXFRMInterfaceEntries, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched_xfrm_interface.go; TestInterfaceSPDCatchAllInstallFailureFailsTheApply), and `unmatched discard` is refused at verify where the dataplane says ahead of time it cannot hold the entry (verifyUnmatchedEnforceable). This window statement is about the IKE engine's SPD alone, and the OSPFv3 entries above are outside it. Outside the window from the first configuration apply to the IKE engine's exit, the IKE engine holds no SPD entry and no SA: applyConfig (internal/component/ike/engine/apply.go) installs the catch-all first and the IKE bypass after it, and on every exit shutdown stops each peer (removing its Child SA), closes the IKE sockets, then releaseSPD removes the catch-all last, and keeps it installed with a warning when any other removal failed (warnSPDLeft, spd_left.go). So in those windows the IKE engine is not acting as an IPsec implementation and no packet is looked up in an SPD-I of its own, a reading the owner accepted on 2026-10-20 (TestRunInstallsNoSPDEntryBeforeTheFirstApply, TestRFC4301FinalEntryIsUnderEveryZeSPDEntry, TestReleaseSPDKeepsTheCatchAllWhenARemovalFails). TestRFC4301InboundClearPacketMatchingNoEntryFollowsTheUnmatchedLeaf shows the installed catch-all deciding an inbound clear packet on XFRM |
| [`RFC4301-5.2-2`](#rfc4301-5.2-2) Then match the packet against the inbound selectors identified by the SAD entry to verify that the received packet is appropriate for the SA via which it was received. (§5.2) | {partial}, Partial test coverage | tested "match the packet against the inbound selectors identified by the SAD entry"; gap "verify that the received packet is appropriate for the SA via which it was received"; internal/component/ike/engine/child.go::installChildDataplane now constructs both complete directional scopes before publishing either state, through internal/component/ike/engine/child_scope.go::childStateScope and dataplane.NewSAStateScope. Native positive and negative records prove single-pair whole and outer-fragmented bare ESP on an ESP-in-UDP state under the production SPD, including the state's selector rejection and a separate explicit SPD DISCARD. Those records do not establish general multi-pair or overlapping per-SA isolation. checkXFRMStateScopeCapabilities retains the full feature-bits 0-3 admission guard; the carried K3 fragment guard is enrolled, and the combined native kernel has compiled and booted with the complete 0xf receipt. Bounded scope, fragment and port-policy packet runs have passed, including a private whole-packet sibling-port cut that triggered the selected test's four own assertions. These observations do not complete the general isolation proof or renew its RFC records: the constrained permitted fragment and context cases now pass on the normal Ze kernel (eight selected packet/helper tests, including all 16 context rows), while separate broader RFC kernel-discrimination debt remains, with no private fault authorization or new C-enforcement credit. General isolation remains owned by plan/release/fixes/spec-rfc4301-architecture-gaps.md; ipsec-dual-form-receive-spd-bypass AC-4 now accepts the required normal Ze-kernel positive, negative and control packet tests for product closure. That acceptance does not remove this partial annotation or grant whole-requirement proof; the remaining RFC discrimination debt stays separate under the existing evidence rules |
| [`RFC4301-7-1`](#rfc4301-7-1) Note: AH and ESP cannot be applied using transport mode to IPv4 packets that are fragments. Only tunnel mode can be employed in such cases. (§4.1) | {gap}, no test | ze's IKE negotiates transport-mode Child SAs for any address family (internal/component/ike/ipsec/traffic_selector.go::parseChildMode), so the rule is reachable over IPv4; on Linux, XFRM reassembles an inbound fragment before its policy check and applies ESP before outbound fragmentation, with no test over the state ze installs, and on the VPP backend (internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp.go) the behaviour is unverified |
| [`RFC4301-4.4.1-3`](#rfc4301-4.4.1-3) Every SPD SHOULD have a nominal, final entry that matches anything that is otherwise unmatched, and discards it. (§4.4.1) | {partial}, Partial test coverage | tested "a nominal, final entry that matches anything that is otherwise unmatched, and discards it"; gap "Every SPD"; the final DISCARD entry is installed and tested only in the XFRM SPDs the IKE engine owns: the node-wide SPD at if_id 0 (installUnmatched, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched.go) and the SPD of each XFRM interface a peer binds with vti bind (interfaceSPDCatchAll, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched_xfrm_interface.go). Two kinds of SPD carry no final entry. The VPP SPDs: internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp_policy.go::vppPolicyInterface refuses a policy bound to no interface and vppBackend.CatchAllSupported answers dataplane.ErrNoNodeWideSPD, so installUnmatched installs nothing on VPP and no SPD that vppBackend.ensureSPD creates and binds receives a final entry. The kernel SPD holding the OSPFv3 RFC 4552 manual-key entries: internal/plugins/ospf/ipsec_install.go::defaultDataplaneSource hands the OSPF installer whichever backend is loaded, and buildIPsecPolicies installs per-interface protocol 89 transport entries with no final entry beneath them, in force with no vpn ipsec block and after the IKE engine releases its SPD. Owned by plan/post-release/features/spec-ipsec-spd-final-discard-vpp-ospf.md |
| [`RFC4301-4.1-10`](#rfc4301-4.1-10) In transport mode, the DSCP value might change en route, but this should not cause problems with respect to IPsec processing since the value is not employed for SA selection and MUST NOT be checked as part of SA/packet validation. (§4.1) | no test | no test carries this requirement id; annotated {lower-layer}: Linux XFRM SA and policy lookup (net/xfrm, the XFRM core linux:CONFIG_XFRM_USER selects); internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmStateFromParams writes no DSCP into the state and xfrmPolicyFromParams none into the selector, so no value Ze installs is read against DSCP |
| [`RFC4301-4.1-11`](#rfc4301-4.1-11) In IPv6, the security protocol header appears after the base IP header and selected extension headers, but may appear before or after destination options; it MUST appear before next layer protocols (e.g., TCP, UDP, Stream Control Transmission Protocol (SCTP)). (§4.1) | no test | no test carries this requirement id; annotated {lower-layer}: Linux XFRM ESP output (net/ipv6/esp6.c), linux:CONFIG_INET6_ESP; internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmStateFromParams installs the ESP state and the kernel output path builds every header, so no value Ze writes decides where the ESP header sits |
| [`RFC4301-4.4.1-13`](#rfc4301-4.4.1-13) Note that when an SPD entry is decorrelated all the resulting entries MUST be linked together, so that all members of the group derived from an individual, SPD entry (prior to decorrelation) can all be placed into caches and into the SAD at the same time. (§4.4.1) | {gap}, no test | no owner decision declines decorrelation. Section 4.4.1 states "This RFC does not require a compliant implementation to make use of decorrelation.", and ze keeps the SPD ordered and never decorrelates it: internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmPolicyFromParams writes each SPD entry as one kernel XFRM policy carrying its priority, and the kernel performs the ordered search, so no decorrelated group exists to link. The obligation stays declared until a scope decision of Thomas's declines decorrelation or ze decorrelates its SPD |
| [`RFC4301-4.4.1.1-2`](#rfc4301-4.4.1.1-2) Note that the Local and Remote ports may not be available in the case of receipt of a fragmented packet or if the port fields have been protected by IPsec (encrypted); thus, a value of OPAQUE also MUST be supported. (§4.4.1.1) | {gap}, no test | the operator SPD list refuses OPAQUE and Ze maps OPAQUE onto no kernel selector yet, although the carried netlink patch now writes explicit masks so exactly port 0 is expressible (Linux limitation LL-NETLINK-XFRM-PORT-MASK, docs/architecture/linux-limitations.md, which records the library limit as carried by that patch); plan/post-release/features/spec-ipsec-opaque-selector-port-mask.md |
| [`RFC4301-4.4.1.1-5`](#rfc4301-4.4.1.1-5) The name used to match this field is communicated during the IKE negotiation in the ID payload. In this context, the initiator's Source IP address (inner IP header in tunnel mode) is bound to the Remote IP address in the SAD entry created by the IKE negotiation. This address overrides the Remote IP address value in the SPD, when the SPD entry is selected in this fashion. All IPsec implementations MUST support this use of names. (§4.4.1.1) | {gap}, no test | no name-valued selector selects an operator SPD entry during IKE; plan/release/fixes/spec-ipsec-spd-name-selection.md |
| [`RFC4301-4.4.1.2-2`](#rfc4301-4.4.1.2-2) Until IKE provides a facility that conveys the semantics that are expressed in the SPD via selector sets (as described below), users MUST NOT include multiple selector sets in a single SPD entry unless the access control intent aligns with the IKE "mix and match" semantics. (§4.4.1.2) | {gap}, no test | no owner decision declines multiple selector sets per SPD entry. RFC 4301 Section 4.4.1.2 says "It is possible to associate multiple protocols (and ports) with a single SA by specifying multiple selector sets for that SA.", a possibility an earlier pass read as an optional feature without a decision of Thomas's behind it. internal/component/ike/ipsec/spd_policy.go::parseSPDPolicy reads one selector set per SPD entry, so no configuration reaches the multiple-set entry this MUST NOT constrains, and no test proves it |
| [`RFC4301-4.4.2.1-13`](#rfc4301-4.4.2.1-13) Stateful fragment checking flag. (§4.4.2.1) | {gap}, no test | no SAD entry Ze installs holds a stateful fragment checking flag, and no config sets one; no spec owns it yet |
| [`RFC4301-4.4.2.1-14`](#rfc4301-4.4.2.1-14) Bypass DF bit (T/F) -- applicable to tunnel mode SAs where both inner and outer headers are IPv4. | [`RFC4301-4.4.2.1-15`](#rfc4301-4.4.2.1-15) DSCP values -- the set of DSCP values allowed for packets carried over this SA. (§4.4.2.1) | {gap}, no test | no SAD entry Ze installs holds a set of DSCP values, so no outbound SA selection by DSCP exists; no spec owns it yet |

(§6) | {gap}, no test | an operator SPD entry can name an ICMP type and code, and the catch-all and Child SA forward policies decide transit ICMP, but the operator list installs in and out policies only, so an administrator cannot write a bypass or discard for transit ICMP on a gateway; a forward-direction projection must first decide how a non-initial fragment, which carries no ICMP type, is classified (Section 7); plan/release/fixes/spec-rfc4301-architecture-gaps.md |
| [`RFC4301-6.2-2`](#rfc4301-6.2-2) IPsec senders and receivers MUST support the following processing for ICMP error messages that are sent and received via SAs. (§6.2) | {gap}, no test | the processing holds for an ICMP error addressed to or generated by Ze (XFRM_STATE_ICMP, XFRM_POLICY_ICMP), and is absent for a forwarded one that arrives on an SA, which the kernel never inspects while Ze's fwd catch-all matches its headers; a forwarded one leaving towards an SA is not mapped under `unmatched discard`, because the out discard catch-all blocks it first; both are Ze-owed designs on the stock kernel, not kernel limits (LL-XFRM-ICMP-TRANSIT, docs/architecture/linux-limitations.md, a design constraint); plan/release/fixes/spec-rfc4301-architecture-gaps.md |
| [`RFC4301-6.2-3`](#rfc4301-6.2-3) If no SA exists that would carry the outbound ICMP message in question, and if no SPD entry would allow carriage of this outbound ICMP error message, then an IPsec implementation MUST map the message to the SA that would carry the return traffic associated with the packet that triggered the ICMP error message. (§6.2) | {partial}, Partial test coverage | tested "if no SPD entry would allow carriage of this outbound ICMP error message"; gap "MUST map the message to the SA that would carry the return traffic associated with the packet that triggered the ICMP error message"; the kernel maps an error into the return SA also when a BYPASS entry would carry it in clear, which this condition excludes, so internal/component/ike/engine/icmp_overlap.go::refuseICMPErrorBypass refuses at commit, by owner decision, `unmatched bypass` and every out or both bypass entry carrying ICMP error types towards the remote selectors of a peer whose icmp-error-check is true and which is not bound to a VTI (a VTI peer's policies carry its if_id, which the kernel's reverse lookup at if_id 0 never finds), and the tags prove that refusal and its accepted controls; Gap is the mapping itself: no namespace test observes an error Ze generates being sent into the return SA (XFRM_POLICY_ICMP), and a forwarded error under `unmatched discard` is blocked by the out catch-all before the kernel maps it, a Ze-owed design on the stock kernel (LL-XFRM-ICMP-TRANSIT, docs/architecture/linux-limitations.md, a design constraint); plan/release/fixes/spec-rfc4301-architecture-gaps.md |
| [`RFC4301-6.2-4`](#rfc4301-6.2-4) If an IPsec implementation receives an inbound ICMP error message on an SA, and the IP and ICMP headers of the message do not match the traffic selectors for the SA, the receiver MUST process the received message in a special fashion. Specifically, the receiver must extract the header of the triggering packet from the ICMP payload, and reverse fields as described above to determine if the packet is consistent with the selectors for the SA via which the ICMP error message was received. (§6.2) | {partial}, Partial test coverage | tested "the receiver MUST process the received message in a special fashion"; gap "If an IPsec implementation receives an inbound ICMP error message on an SA"; the split is by case, not by clause: Tested covers an ICMP error addressed to Ze, which the kernel delivers when its embedded header, reversed, matches the SA's inbound policy and drops when it does not, on the state internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmStateFromParams installs with XFRM_STATE_ICMP (TestICMPErrorPayloadHeaderCheckedOnLocalDelivery); Gap is a forwarded ICMP error that arrives on an SA, which is dropped unread because Ze's fwd catch-all is a policy and so keeps xfrm_in_fwd_icmp from running; Ze owes the fix, the node-wide fwd catch-all moved to the netns XFRM default policy and the fwd interface catch-all removed (LL-XFRM-ICMP-TRANSIT, docs/architecture/linux-limitations.md, a design constraint and not a kernel limit); plan/release/fixes/spec-rfc4301-architecture-gaps.md |
| [`RFC4301-6.2-5`](#rfc4301-6.2-5) If the packet fails this check, the IPsec implementation MUST NOT forwarded the ICMP message to the destination. (§6.2) | {partial}, Partial test coverage | tested "MUST NOT forwarded the ICMP message to the destination"; gap "If the packet fails this check"; the split is by case, not by clause: Tested covers an ICMP error addressed to Ze, which is not delivered when its embedded header, reversed, matches no inbound policy of the SA and is delivered when it does, on the state internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmStateFromParams installs with XFRM_STATE_ICMP; Gap is a forwarded ICMP error, for which the check never runs: Ze's fwd catch-all drops it before xfrm_in_fwd_icmp, so it is not forwarded either, but no namespace test observes that drop, a testing gap (LL-XFRM-ICMP-TRANSIT, docs/architecture/linux-limitations.md, a design constraint); plan/release/fixes/spec-rfc4301-architecture-gaps.md |
| [`RFC4301-7.2-1`](#rfc4301-7.2-1) Receivers MUST perform a minimum offset check on IPv4 (non-initial) fragments to protect against overlapping fragment attacks when SAs of this type are employed. (§7.2) | {gap}, no test | the obligation binds a receiver that employs SAs carrying non-initial fragments (approach #2 of Section 7.2), and internal/component/ike/engine/child.go::childPolicies builds one policy pair per negotiated selector pair and never such an SA, so no minimum offset check is tied to an SA of this type; no owner decision declines the non-initial-fragment SA, so under the owner rule that an absent requirement of an implemented RFC is owed work this is a gap and not an exclusion |
| [`RFC4301-7.2-2`](#rfc4301-7.2-2) Specific port (or ICMP type/code or Mobility Header type) selector values will be used to define SAs to carry initial fragments and non-fragmented packets. This approach can be used if a user or administrator wants to create one or more tunnel mode SAs between the same Local/Remote addresses that discriminate based on port (or ICMP type/code or Mobility Header type) fields. These SAs MUST have non-trivial protocol selector values, otherwise approach #1 above MUST be used. (§7.2) | {gap}, no test | the obligation constrains SAs that carry initial fragments and non-fragmented packets beside a non-initial-fragment SA (approach #2 of Section 7.2), and internal/component/ike/engine/child.go::childPolicies builds no fragment-carrying SA, so Ze never writes the protocol selector this sentence constrains; no owner decision declines that approach, so under the owner rule that an absent requirement of an implemented RFC is owed work this is a gap and not an exclusion |
| [`RFC4301-7.3-1`](#rfc4301-7.3-1) Implementations that will transmit non-initial fragments on a tunnel mode SA that makes use of non-trivial port (or ICMP type/code or MH type) selectors MUST notify a peer via the IKE NOTIFY NON_FIRST_FRAGMENTS_ALSO payload (§7.3) | {gap}, no test | Ze never sends NON_FIRST_FRAGMENTS_ALSO; plan/spec-ipsec-non-first-fragments-also.md |
| [`RFC4301-7.3-2`](#rfc4301-7.3-2) The peer MUST reject this proposal if it will not accept non-initial fragments in this context. (§7.3) | {gap}, no test | Ze never parses or answers NON_FIRST_FRAGMENTS_ALSO; plan/spec-ipsec-non-first-fragments-also.md |
| [`RFC4301-8.1-2`](#rfc4301-8.1-2) This means that it MUST be possible to configure the implementation's treatment of the DF bit (set, clear, copy from inner header) for each SA. (§8.1) | {partial}, Partial test coverage | tested "clear, copy from inner header"; gap "set"; copy and clear are configurable per peer (df-bit), and the tags prove each reaches the SA on Linux XFRM and VPP and the wire, and that a treatment a dataplane cannot apply is refused at commit and again at install rather than installed as another; Gap is DF set, which internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmDFFlag and internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp.go::vppDFError refuse because neither the XFRM state nor VPP's ESP encrypt path writes a set DF bit on an ESP tunnel header (LL-XFRM-OUTER-DF-SET-DSCP, docs/architecture/linux-limitations.md, a design constraint and not a kernel limit: an nftables rewrite of the outer header could write it); the owner decided that refusal is the behaviour for the first release, with a Ze kernel patch after it (plan/post-release/kernel/spec-xfrm-outer-df-set-dscp.md), and it is never counted conformant; plan/release/fixes/spec-rfc4301-architecture-gaps.md |

## Test status

A test reads not verified when no record shows it failing after a deliberate fault. Until then, the test has not been shown to detect a fault in the required behavior.

### [`RFC4301-4.2-1`](#rfc4301-4.2-1)

Note: A compliant implementation MUST NOT allow instantiation of an ESP SA that employs both NULL encryption and no integrity algorithm. (§4.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmStateFromParams moved. Rejudged after xfrmStateFromParams acquired the xfrmStateScopeFromParams call. RFC 4301 Section 4.2 says: Note: A compliant implementation MUST NOT allow instantiation of an ESP SA that employs both NULL encryption and no integrity algorithm. The scope projection adds no transform and does not bypass planStateAlgos or xfrmAuthName. Every non-AEAD ESP state still requests an Auth transform, and an empty or none algorithm is refused before a state is returned. TestRFC4301ESPNullEncryptionWithIntegrityIsInstantiated asserts the null cipher and HMAC-SHA-256 transform; TestRFC4301ESPNullEncryptionWithoutIntegrityIsRefused requires an error and nil state for absent and none integrity. The VPP negative requires ErrNotSupported and no SAD message, with a successful valid-SA control. The OSPF configuration pair rejects absent integrity and accepts SHA-256. The paired boundary assertions distinguish the forbidden combination from legitimate integrity-only ESP.

### [`RFC4301-4.4.2-1`](#rfc4301-4.4.2-1)

For each of the selectors defined in Section 4.4.1.1, the entry for an inbound SA in the SAD MUST be initially populated with the value or values negotiated at the time the SA was created. (§4.4.2)

Audit verdict: weak (the tests pass over code that does not enforce the requirement), shifted: internal/component/ike/engine/child_test.go::TestChildSAInboundPolicyUsesNegotiatedTS, internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_sad_selector_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301TransportModeInboundSADEntryDropsPacketsOutsideItsSelectors, internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_sad_selector_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301TransportModeInboundSADEntryDropsPacketsOutsideItsSelectors#2, internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_sad_selector_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301TunnelModeInboundSelectorsAreEnforcedByTheRequirePolicy, internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_sad_selector_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301TunnelModeInboundSelectorsAreEnforcedByTheRequirePolicy#2 moved. Rejudged the whole Section 4.4.2 sentence: For each of the selectors defined in Section 4.4.1.1, the entry for an inbound SA in the SAD MUST be initially populated with the value or values negotiated at the time the SA was created. The tagged projection tests preserve address, protocol and port values for one negotiated pair, and the tunnel/transport packet tests prove matching traffic delivered and nonmatching traffic refused. The revised tunnel negative now correctly requires XfrmInStateMismatch exactly one, with both SPD counters unchanged, for the same source, port and protocol violations. Those assertions remain useful, but every tagged fixture uses one pair. installChildDataplane binds a tunnel state only when tunnelStateSelectorSupported accepts its policy set; TestTunnelMultiplePairsKeepTheirPolicySet explicitly keeps inbound.Sel nil for multiple pairs. The tagged set therefore cannot establish the quoted values obligation for an SA negotiated with several pairs. A second row quoting the same obligation does not narrow this unannotated row. The earlier enforced verdict overstated the tagged coverage; no test or tag is removed.

### [`RFC4301-4.4.2-2`](#rfc4301-4.4.2-2)

the entry for an inbound SA in the SAD MUST be initially populated with the value or values negotiated at the time the SA was created. (§4.4.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-26 against HEAD c8584247bb (independent judge, clean clone, after a8797fd0c2, c8d3e41036, 1502b3034b). Section 4.4.2: "For each of the selectors defined in Section 4.4.1.1, the entry for an inbound SA in the SAD MUST be initially populated with the value or values negotiated at the time the SA was created." narrowChildSelectors changed in two places: substituteVirtualIP now drops a proposed TSi that does not contain the lease instead of replacing it (refusing with TS_UNACCEPTABLE when none is left), and the transport single-address filter is gated on transportSelectorsInPlay, which is false for a pool-bound peer whose transport request decideResponderTransportMode declined. Neither changes what is recorded: sa.NegotiatedPairs is still exactly the answered set (closedSubset of narrowed), and childPolicies/inboundStateSelector (unchanged) build the SAD selector from it, so SAD = negotiated holds on every path including the leased one. recordInitiatorSelectors gained answerNamesOnlyLeases before the ceiling, which only refuses; an accepted answer is still recorded as answered. Tagged carriers (TestRFC4301TransportStateSelectorIsTheNegotiatedPair, TestRFC4301MixedProtocolPairInstallsTheIntersection, TestRFC4301AuthResponseWithoutTSIsRefused, TestRFC4301InitiatorInstallsAnAddressRangeExactly, TestRFC4301InitiatorRefusesAnAnswerItCannotInstallExactly, TestRFC4301TransportInitiatorRefusesSeveralPairs, TestRFC4301InitiatorInstallsEveryAnsweredCombination) PASS at HEAD (./le job run, scoped -run). ./le rfc discriminate id RFC4301-4.4.2-2: ten mutant records, none stale, none unproven. Verdict stands.

### [`RFC4301-5.2-1`](#rfc4301-5.2-1)

Since the SPD-I is just a part of the SPD, if a packet that is looked up in the SPD-I cannot be matched to an entry there, then the packet MUST be discarded. (§4.4.1)

Audit verdict: unimplemented (no code path enforces the requirement), fresh. Rejudged against RFC 4301 Section 4.4.1: Since the SPD-I is just a part of the SPD, if a packet that is looked up in the SPD-I cannot be matched to an entry there, then the packet MUST be discarded. applyConfig still establishes the final disposition before operator policies or peers on first apply, and shutdown stops peers before releaseSPD. installUnmatched now owns the namespace FWD default through ForwardDefaultInstaller and omits explicit FWD catch-alls for that backend; installXFRMInterfaceEntries likewise omits redundant interface FWD catch-alls. IN and OUT final entries remain explicit. releaseSPD retains final disposition while warnSPDLeft reports a remaining entry, then removeUnmatched releases the FWD default after successful policy removal. These changes preserve enforcement for the IKE-owned XFRM SPD but do not supply the two declared absent cases: VPP refuses node-wide SPDs and runs unmatched bypass without a final entry, and OSPF manual-key entries can exist without the IKE engine and without their own final entry. vppPolicyInterface and defaultDataplaneSource confirm those boundaries. The explicit gap remains and no conformance claim is upgraded.

No test carries RFC4301-5.2-1, so there is no linked test.

### [`RFC4301-5.2-2`](#rfc4301-5.2-2)

Then match the packet against the inbound selectors identified by the SAD entry to verify that the received packet is appropriate for the SA via which it was received. (§5.2)

Records cover only part of this requirement: tested "match the packet against the inbound selectors identified by the SAD entry"; gap "verify that the received packet is appropriate for the SA via which it was received"; internal/component/ike/engine/child.go::installChildDataplane now constructs both complete directional scopes before publishing either state, through internal/component/ike/engine/child_scope.go::childStateScope and dataplane.NewSAStateScope. Native positive and negative records prove single-pair whole and outer-fragmented bare ESP on an ESP-in-UDP state under the production SPD, including the state's selector rejection and a separate explicit SPD DISCARD. Those records do not establish general multi-pair or overlapping per-SA isolation. checkXFRMStateScopeCapabilities retains the full feature-bits 0-3 admission guard; the carried K3 fragment guard is enrolled, and the combined native kernel has compiled and booted with the complete 0xf receipt. Bounded scope, fragment and port-policy packet runs have passed, including a private whole-packet sibling-port cut that triggered the selected test's four own assertions. These observations do not complete the general isolation proof or renew its RFC records: the constrained permitted fragment and context cases now pass on the normal Ze kernel (eight selected packet/helper tests, including all 16 context rows), while separate broader RFC kernel-discrimination debt remains, with no private fault authorization or new C-enforcement credit. General isolation remains owned by plan/release/fixes/spec-rfc4301-architecture-gaps.md; ipsec-dual-form-receive-spd-bypass AC-4 now accepts the required normal Ze-kernel positive, negative and control packet tests for product closure. That acceptance does not remove this partial annotation or grant whole-requirement proof; the remaining RFC discrimination debt stays separate under the existing evidence rules; they do not verify the whole requirement.

Audit verdict: partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), stale-unit: internal/component/ike/engine/child.go::installChildDataplane moved. Independent one-ID source rejudgment after the closing-spec citation and obsolete fixture-status reason edits, against RFC 4301 Section 5.2 step 4: "Then match the packet against the inbound selectors identified by the SAD entry to verify that the received packet is appropriate for the SA via which it was received." Tested: "match the packet against the inbound selectors identified by the SAD entry". Gap: "verify that the received packet is appropriate for the SA via which it was received". These locate one sentence; the split remains by tested case, not independent matching and verification obligations. All nine tagged covers across six bodies were reread. The boundary pair pins inbound direction, source prefix, exact port and transform template and refuses an unrepresentable port mask. The single-pair transport fixture compares the selector passed for installation field by field and refuses unrepresentable multiple pairs. The tunnel and transport packet bodies require valid delivery controls and exact own-SA mismatch rejection for the source, port and protocol inputs they exercise; the ICMP protocol cases are counter observations, not listener-delivery claims. The bare-ESP body reads the production first-apply SPD, checks whole fast-path and outer-fragmented complete-path admission/refusal on one ESP-in-UDP-templated SA, exact counters, an independent in-scope SPD DISCARD and delivery after its exact removal. Outer ESP fragmentation is not protected inner-fragment context proof. The gap-context Producer remains internal/component/ike/engine/child.go::installChildDataplane, which constructs both directional scopes through childStateScope before either state is published. childStateScope preserves the expanded selector records, requires a transport singleton and retains noninitial permission false. checkXFRMStateScopeCapabilities still requires the exact ABI, selector limit and all four required feature bits. The earlier constrained-fixture blocker is resolved: actual Claude native packet run 15823 on the normal Ze kernel passed all eight selected packet/helper tests with no FAIL or SKIP; the full job-ipsec-fragment-normal-packets-472ed847.log, independently read in its recovered local normal-packets.log, shows all 16 expected context rows and the following authenticated whole controls, permitted compliant-fragment delivery and noncompliant-fragment exact (StateMismatch, NoPols, PolBlock) = (1, 0, 0). Check, command and lab exits were zero, interrupted was false and the VM stopped. These normal observed fixtures resolve that implementation/test precondition; they do not add tags or claims to these nine covers, establish general RFC kernel-discrimination or authorize private fault preparation. No new C-enforcement discrimination is inferred. The row therefore retains its distinct broader RFC proof debt and partial verdict. Retain the partial classification, tested/gap selectors and zero whole-requirement credit; broader RFC evidence remains with plan/release/fixes/spec-rfc4301-architecture-gaps.md. The reason now cites the bare historical ipsec-dual-form-receive-spd-bypass stem because its closing spec is being removed. Its normal Ze-kernel product-closure acceptance does not change global RFC proof rules or turn existing Go-route dependency records into C-enforcement discrimination. No tag, claim, normative quote, tested/gap split or functional behavior changed. This rejudgment incorporates the already observed bounded normal fixture resolution; it adds no new run or fault-preparation approval.

### [`RFC4301-4.1-6`](#rfc4301-4.1-6)

If an IPsec implementation supports multicast, then it MUST support multicast SAs using the algorithm below for mapping inbound IPsec datagrams to SAs. (§4.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Judged on the path where Ze has multicast SAs (R24): the RFC 4552 manual OSPFv3 installer (buildIPsecInterfaceSAs/buildIPsecSA) run through onInterfaceUp over the real XFRM backend in a rootless user+net namespace; the lookup is netlink XFRM_MSG_GETSA against the kernel SAD (xfrm_state_lookup, the function XFRM input uses), not a mock (run by the judge 2026-09-30: child ran, not skipped). Positive: ff02::5 and ff02::6 share one SPI and each (group, SPI) lookup returns that group's own SA, i.e. step 2 (SPI + destination) of the section 4.1 search resolves an Any-Source Multicast group SA. Negative: after a control lookup, ff02::5 under an unconfigured SPI and the configured SPI to ff02::1 map to no SA (step 3 fails, packet has no SA). Step 1 (SPI+dst+src) is vacuous: Ze builds no source-specific multicast SA. Caveat recorded, not a finding against the tests: the ff02::1 case rests on Linux identifying the fe80:: unicast SA by (dst, SPI, proto) rather than the section 4.1 SPI(+protocol) identifier, so a literal step 3 would match that unicast SA; the stray-SPI case alone carries the negative without that reading. Records: revert buildIPsecSA, both polarities observed red.

### [`RFC4301-4.4.1-3`](#rfc4301-4.4.1-3)

Every SPD SHOULD have a nominal, final entry that matches anything that is otherwise unmatched, and discards it. (§4.4.1)

Records cover only part of this requirement: tested "a nominal, final entry that matches anything that is otherwise unmatched, and discards it"; gap "Every SPD"; the final DISCARD entry is installed and tested only in the XFRM SPDs the IKE engine owns: the node-wide SPD at if_id 0 (installUnmatched, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched.go) and the SPD of each XFRM interface a peer binds with vti bind (interfaceSPDCatchAll, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched_xfrm_interface.go). Two kinds of SPD carry no final entry. The VPP SPDs: internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp_policy.go::vppPolicyInterface refuses a policy bound to no interface and vppBackend.CatchAllSupported answers dataplane.ErrNoNodeWideSPD, so installUnmatched installs nothing on VPP and no SPD that vppBackend.ensureSPD creates and binds receives a final entry. The kernel SPD holding the OSPFv3 RFC 4552 manual-key entries: internal/plugins/ospf/ipsec_install.go::defaultDataplaneSource hands the OSPF installer whichever backend is loaded, and buildIPsecPolicies installs per-interface protocol 89 transport entries with no final entry beneath them, in force with no vpn ipsec block and after the IKE engine releases its SPD. Owned by plan/post-release/features/spec-ipsec-spd-final-discard-vpp-ospf.md; they do not verify the whole requirement.

Audit verdict: partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), stale-unit: internal/component/ike/engine/apply.go::applyConfig, internal/component/ike/engine/apply.go::releaseSPD, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched.go::installUnmatched, internal/component/ike/engine/unmatched_xfrm_interface.go::installXFRMInterfaceEntries moved. Section 4.4.1: "Every SPD SHOULD have a nominal, final entry that matches anything that is otherwise unmatched, and discards it." Re-judged 2026-11-12 (independent auditor) because apply.go::applyConfig changed: the one hunk adds warnICMPOverlapAtStart(cfg, s.log) beside warnImplicitDiscardAtStart in the applyStartup branch and widens its comment. warnICMPOverlapAtStart (icmp_overlap.go) only logs each refusal icmpOverlapRefusals answers; it and both refusal functions read cfg and write nothing, so no SPD entry, ranking or disposition moved, and the call sits after the node catch-all, guard, IKE bypass, interface entries and prune, with no new return path. Tagged units are byte-identical to the previous judgement and ./le rfc check names applyConfig alone, raising no record complaint for this id. Tested: "a nominal, final entry that matches anything that is otherwise unmatched, and discards it", in the XFRM SPDs the IKE engine owns. Node-wide if_id 0 SPD: TestRFC4301DefaultFinalSPDEntryDiscards (installUnmatched builds in/out/fwd wildcard DISCARD at PriorityUnmatched by default), TestRFC4301FinalEntryIsUnderEveryZeSPDEntry (every write replayed, catch-all present and DISCARD whenever another Ze entry is live), negative TestRFC4301InboundClearPacketIsDiscardedByTheDefaultFinalEntry (kernel XfrmInPolBlock +1, packet not delivered). Per-interface if_id SPDs: TestInterfaceSPDCatchAllEntries (interfaceSPDCatchAll: DISCARD at the if_id, below PriorityChildSA, unnarrowed), TestApplyInstallsInterfaceSPDCatchAllAndGuard (applyConfig on applyReload, so the new startup-only call is not on its path; asserts the exact interface catch-all set, five exemptions, node catch-all DISCARD and the guard), TestReleaseRemovesInterfaceSPDAndGuard, TestRFC4301InterfaceSPDDiscardsUnmatchedInKernel (XfrmOutPolBlock rises at the if_id, Child SA policy outranks it), negative TestInterfaceSPDCatchAllInstallFailureFailsTheApply. They assert the exact DISCARD action and ranking, not a floor. Each cover carries a discrimination record (xfrmPolicyAction BLOCK->ALLOW, parseUnmatched disabled, shutdown releaseSPD-before-stop, planXFRMInterfaceEntries catchAll[:0], interfaceSPDCatchAll DISCARD->BYPASS, installXFRMInterfaceEntries noFinalEntry dropped, releaseSPD warnSPDLeft && false); none breaks applyConfig, so the edit stales none, and the releaseSPD record the previous note called owed is now present. Gap: "Every SPD". Accurate at the producers: internal/component/ike/dataplane/vpp_policy.go::vppPolicyInterface refuses IfIndex <= 0 and vppBackend.CatchAllSupported answers ErrNoNodeWideSPD, so no VPP SPD gets a final entry; defaultDataplaneSource plus buildIPsecPolicies install the OSPFv3 proto-89 transport entries with no final entry of their own, in force with no vpn ipsec block and after releaseSPD. {partial} annotation accurate; the whole requirement remains unproven.

### [`RFC4301-4.4.1-4`](#rfc4301-4.4.1-4)

Thus, a user or administrator MUST be able to order the entries to express a desired access control policy. (§4.4.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-27 (independent judge, after 2f36373081). RFC 4301 Section 4.4.1: "The ordering requirement arises because entries often will overlap due to the presence of (non-trivial) ranges as values for selectors. Thus, a user or administrator MUST be able to order the entries to express a desired access control policy." The STALE unit TestSpdUnstatedOrderTakesTheDefaultRank changed only by newRekeyedChild gaining its mode argument (child.Mode, the value the constructor previously copied from old.Mode); Mode does not reach childPairPolicy's priority, which still resolves child.PolicyPriority with zero mapped to PriorityChildSA, and newRekeyedChild still copies old.PolicyPriority. Assertions unchanged: operator rank reaches both directions and orders two overlapping 0.0.0.0/0 peers (positive; its revert record on childPolicies no longer verifies, producer-changed, and is owed a re-record by the author); unstated order is never 0 and takes the default rank (negative); an order capturing IKE is refused (engine and ipsec; the ipsec revert record on spd_policy.go validate verifies). All tagged units PASS at 95efd5421d. Observed this audit: disabling the zero-to-default branch in childPairPolicy turns TestSpdUnstatedOrderTakesTheDefaultRank red (rank 0 reached the SPD). The unit's rekey half runs on rank 0 and cannot catch a rekey that drops the operator's rank; dropping that copy in newRekeyedChild is caught by the untagged TestOrderedTransportChildRekeyAfterIKERekey, observed red this audit. Verdict stands.

### [`RFC4301-4.1-12`](#rfc4301-4.1-12)

The indication of whether source and destination address matching is required to map inbound IPsec traffic to SAs MUST be set either as a side effect of manual SA configuration or via negotiation using an SA management protocol, e.g., IKE or Group Domain of Interpretation (GDOI) [RFC3547]. (§4.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged pass 6. The unit now asserts what Linux actually reads for inbound source matching on a transport-mode SA: the state selector x->sel (xfrm_selector_match in __xfrm_policy_check), read back from the kernel for the ff02::5 and ff02::6 states found by (group, SPI), with source ::/0; a control AH state installed with selector source fe80::2/128 is read back first as a /128, so the read-back discriminates a pinned source from the wildcard. The props-source :: assertion is kept and correctly described as the outbound address check, not claimed. Doc comment, PREVENTS line and failure text now name the right mechanism. Producer read: buildIPsecSA sets Sel.Src to ospfWildcardNet(). Each probe runs in its own user+net namespace (no cross-test state). Revert record of buildIPsecSA observed red; author's overlay pinning Sel.Src also went red. single-polarity holds: multicast SAs exist only as RFC 4552 manual config, which always sets the indication, so no refusal path exists. Full ospf package -race green.

### [`RFC4301-4.1-9`](#rfc4301-4.1-9)

The receiver MUST process the packets from the different SAs without prejudice. (§4.1)

Audit verdict: weak (the tests pass over code that does not enforce the requirement), stale-unit: internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_boundary_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301BoundarySADEntryCarriesWhatTheKernelLooksUp moved. Rejudged Section 4.1: The receiver MUST process the packets from the different SAs without prejudice. The next sentence expressly binds both transport and tunnel mode. TestRFC4301ParallelSAsWithOneSelectorAreEachReceived drives two genuine tunnel Child SAs with one shared selector, sends first/second/first and requires all deliveries. The updated negative sends the unchanged outside-source packet on each SA and requires no delivery and exactly one receiving-state mismatch per SA, with both SPD counters unchanged; it therefore preserves that tunnel proof after state-selector binding. The SAD boundary pair separately keeps distinct SPIs and refuses unbuildable states. These tests establish useful tunnel-mode behavior but never exercise two transport-mode SAs: parallelChildPair always leaves transport disabled. The earlier whole-requirement enforced verdict therefore claims more than the tagged fixtures establish for the two modes the RFC explicitly names. Tests and tags are retained; a transport counterpart is owed.
