### [`RFC4301-4.4.1-6`](#rfc4301-4.4.1-6)

SPD-S: For traffic that is to be protected using IPsec, the entry consists of the values of the selectors that apply to the traffic to be protected via AH or ESP, controls on how to create SAs based on these selectors, and the parameters needed to effect this protection (e.g., algorithms, modes, etc.). (§4.4.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-30 by an independent auditor after c41ebbaf93 moved the esp-group existence check from the deleted ValidateGroupRefs to the YANG leafref the commit path runs. RFC 4301 Section 4.4.1 re-read in rfc/full/rfc4301.txt: "SPD-S: For traffic that is to be protected using IPsec, the entry consists of the values of the selectors that apply to the traffic to be protected via AH or ESP, controls on how to create SAs based on these selectors, and the parameters needed to effect this protection (e.g., algorithms, modes, etc.)." Q1 both units assert the three parts of the entry, which is what the RFC says. Q2 TestRFC4301SPDSEntryCarriesSelectorsSAControlsAndProtection parses one site-to-site peer through ParseIPsecConfig, requires config.ValidateCustomSections (referenceRefusals) to raise no esp-group/name refusal, and reads back the selector prefixes, the SA creation controls (connection-type, remote address, esp-group) and the protection parameters (mode transport, aes256/sha256), each compared exactly; TestRFC4301SPDSEntryRefusesAMissingOrUnusablePart breaks one part per case (undefined esp-group now refused by the leafref with 'no name "absent" exists at "../../../esp-group/name"', mode beet, unknown connection-type, a /33 selector) and requires a refusal naming that part. Revert records on config.go::parseSiteToSitePeer verify for both polarities. Q3 each negative case changes one leaf of an otherwise complete entry. Q4 all three parts of the entry are covered. The change replaced the producer of one refusal and kept every input and expected part, so the verdict stays enforced.

### [`RFC4301-4.4.1-10`](#rfc4301-4.4.1-10)

If the SPD is not decorrelated, caching is not allowed and an ordered search of SPD MUST be performed to verify that inbound traffic arriving on an SA is consistent with the access control policy expressed in the SPD. (§4.4.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_ordered_spd_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301OrderedInboundSearchAdmitsOnTheFirstRankedEntry, internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_parallel_sa_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301ParallelSAsAreHeldToTheOneSharedPolicy moved. Re-judged 2026-11-10 (independent judge, authored none of spec-rfc4301-architecture-gaps). The stale unit TestRFC4301BoundarySPDEntryIsRefusedRatherThanWidened gained an unknown-mode entry with no tunnel endpoints, so only the mode check stands between it and a built policy, and a second outside-mask port (0x0100 under 0x00ff); its failures moved from Fatalf to Errorf per case. Its RFC4301-4.4.1-10 negative claim narrowed from "a refused entry never reaches the kernel's ordered search at any priority" to "an entry whose mode is neither tunnel nor transport is refused and no policy is built for the kernel's ordered search", which is what the two mode cases assert (error and nil policy). The narrowed claim owes a fresh record. The ordered search itself stays proven both ways by the stack units TestRFC4301OrderedInboundSearchAdmitsOnTheFirstRankedEntry and TestRFC4301OrderedInboundSearchRefusesOnTheFirstRankedDiscard, unchanged. Verdict stands. Prior judgement: Ordered search proven at the stack, both ways, over real XFRM in a rootless netns with two overlapping inbound entries whose kernel priorities the tests read back. Positive TestRFC4301OrderedInboundSearchAdmitsOnTheFirstRankedEntry: Child SA entry (priority 2000, allow) over the inbound catch-all DISCARD (2^32-1, block); an in-selector packet on the SA is delivered with XfrmInPolBlock +0, an out-of-selector packet meets only the catch-all and is dropped (+1). Negative TestRFC4301OrderedInboundSearchRefusesOnTheFirstRankedDiscard: an operator inbound DISCARD (spdPolicyParams, order 1000, read back as 1000 block) covering the Child SA selector drops an in-selector packet on the SA (XfrmInPolBlock +1, not delivered) although the later PROTECT template matches. Also: TestRFC4301ParallelSAs* (verification after decryption, XfrmInNoPols) and the SPD boundary units (priority carried). Minor: orderedInboundPriority returns the first kernel policy with the src/dst strings, which in the negative could be the Child SA entry; the Fatalf then fails loud, never passes wrongly. Re-judged 2026-09-30 (pass 3): the tagged units changed only by tag lines (RFC4301-4.4-1 and 4.4.2.1-1 lines deleted from the dataplane boundary units, RFC4301-5.2-2 lines added to the engine sad_selector units); bodies identical, verdict unchanged. Re-judged 2026-09-30 (leftovers): the two dataplane SPD boundary units changed only by tag lines (RFC4301-5.1-1 and RFC4301-5.2-3 lines, rows retired as lead-ins, moved to RFC4301-5.1-4 and RFC4301-5.2-2); bodies identical to HEAD, judge ran both: PASS; verdict unchanged.

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-11 by an independent judge after the fix round (2691e815b1) added the disposition pair. RFC 4301 4.4.1, under "The SPD MUST permit a user or administrator to specify policy entries as follows:": "SPD-I: For inbound traffic that is to be bypassed or discarded, the entry consists of the values of the selectors that apply to the traffic to be bypassed or discarded." The engine units judged before (TestSPDPolicyMirrorsTheInboundSelector, TestRFC4301BypassReturnTrafficHasAnSPDIEntry, TestRFC4301BypassSPDIEntryIsNeverAnUnmirroredCopy) are unchanged and carry the selector half: the inbound entry holds the mirrored selectors of the traffic, never an unmirrored copy (records on spdPolicyParams). The added positive TestRFC4301SPDPermitsEachDispositionInEachDatabase carries the 'administrator can specify' half: bypass and discard with direction in and both parse through ParseIPsecConfig to the exact SPActionBypass/SPActionDiscard and SPDDirIn/SPDDirBoth; its claim names disposition and direction only, which is what it asserts. The added negative TestRFC4301SPDRefusesADispositionItDoesNotCarry refuses an absent action (not read as PROTECT, the SPAction zero value), protect, an unknown action and an unknown direction, each by its exact message. Revert records on ipsec/spd_policy.go::parseSPDPolicy for both.

### [`RFC4301-4.4.1.1-5`](#rfc4301-4.4.1.1-5)

The name used to match this field is communicated during the IKE negotiation in the ID payload. In this context, the initiator's Source IP address (inner IP header in tunnel mode) is bound to the Remote IP address in the SAD entry created by the IKE negotiation. This address overrides the Remote IP address value in the SPD, when the SPD entry is selected in this fashion. All IPsec implementations MUST support this use of names. (§4.4.1.1)

### [`RFC4301-4.4.1.2-1`](#rfc4301-4.4.1.2-1)

One to N selector sets that correspond to the "condition" for applying a particular IPsec action. (§4.4.1.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independent source rejudgment of RFC 4301 Section 4.4.1.2, including the full SPD-entry description: "One to N selector sets that correspond to the "condition" for applying a particular IPsec action." parseSPDPolicy constructs one set of local/remote prefixes, protocol and ports. N=1 meets this structural item; this does not establish multi-selector negotiation or the separately allocated users-MUST-NOT rule. Both tagged tests were read with holdsSelector: HoldsOneSelectorSet parses an operator tree and asserts exact local/remote prefixes, protocol 17, local port 53 and remote ANY; CannotCarryASecondSelectorSet counts four scalar prefix/port fields and rejects selector-bearing collections. Its reflection guard does not exhaust hypothetical recursively nested future models. The single-positive annotation is justified by this representation's fixed one-set cardinality, not by absence of all configuration error paths. The current exhaustive-ignore directives only document deliberately narrow reflection switches; assertions and selector construction remain substantive. Verdict enforced. No tests or discrimination replay executed by this auditor.

### [`RFC4301-4.4.2.1-1`](#rfc4301-4.4.2.1-1)

Security Parameter Index (SPI): a 32-bit value selected by the receiving end of an SA to uniquely identify the SA. (§4.4.2.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), shifted: internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_sad_items_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301SADItemSPIIsTheNegotiatedValue, internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_sad_items_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301SADItemZeroSPIIsRefused moved. Row now quotes the SPI item alone (split, Correction 2026-09-30). Positive TestRFC4301SADItemSPIIsTheNegotiatedValue: inbound and outbound SAD entries carry the SPI given, unchanged. Negative TestRFC4301SADItemZeroSPIIsRefused: SPI 0 (RFC 4303 section 2.1: reserved, MUST NOT be sent on the wire, so it identifies no SA) builds no state; the refusal is the D-8 fix in xfrmStateFromParams. Unit-level over the builder; both records observed red.

### [`RFC4301-4.4.2.1-5`](#rfc4301-4.4.2.1-5)

Sequence Counter Overflow: a flag indicating whether overflow of the sequence number counter should generate an auditable event and prevent transmission of additional packets on the SA, or whether rollover is permitted. (§4.4.2.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), shifted: internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_sad_items_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301SADItemSequenceCounterOverflowIsNeverRollover moved. Single-polarity positive accepted: no config leaf or negotiation offers rollover or ESN. The unit builds ESP and AH SAs in both directions through xfrmStateFromParams and asserts OSeqMayWrap and ESN unset, so the kernel (xfrm_replay_overflow) audits the overflow and stops transmission. Would go red if the producer set rollover. Record: revert xfrmStateFromParams, observed red. Judge finding (not fixed): the stop-not-rollover decision is the zero value of netlink.XfrmState.OSeqMayWrap and xfrmStateFromParams never names it; under the principles rule a zero another component relies on is a guard that must be named (explicit assignment with the RFC quote). The test pins it, so it cannot vanish silently, but the producer still owes the named guard.

| Polarity | Test | Kind and tier | Test status |
|---|---|---|---|
| positive | [`TestRFC4301SADItemSequenceCounterOverflowIsNeverRollover`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_sad_items_linux_test.go#L280) | unit/verify | revert, producer-changed (the producer's behavior changed since the break was applied to it) |

### [`RFC4301-4.4.2.1-11`](#rfc4301-4.4.2.1-11)

Lifetime of this SA: a time interval after which an SA must be replaced with a new SA (and new SPI) or terminated, plus an indication of which of these actions should occur. (§4.4.2.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independent source rejudgment after the comment-only errcheck annotation on the hard-expiry timeout join. RFC 4301 section 4.4.2.1 says: Lifetime of this SA: a time interval after which an SA must be replaced with a new SA (and new SPI) or terminated, plus an indication of which of these actions should occur. All four tagged units were re-read against newLifetimeState, softExpired, hardExpired and maintainSA, including its rekey trigger and cleanup. The interval pair pins configured duration, replace-before-terminate, neither action before expiry and termination at the hard boundary. The owner-loop pair isolates soft and hard expiry: soft initiates CREATE_CHILD_SA with a new nonzero inbound SPI for the old Child SA and no timeout; hard returns errTimeout, leaves no pending rekey or Child SA, removes the old inbound SPI and sends no request. Removing termination or swapping the actions violates these exact assertions. The edited timeout path still fails unconditionally; no input, assertion or RFC claim changed. Prior proof provenance retained: the existing discrimination record carries revert observations for maintainSA and newLifetimeState; the previous audit additionally reported the author's soft/hard-swap overlay turning both action tests red. Those are historical observations, not executions by this judge. Discrimination records are unchanged. No runtime rerun, build, lint or RFC check was performed for this source-only rejudgment.

|---|---|---|---|
| negative | [`TestRFC4301SADLifetimeHardTimeTerminatesTheSA`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_sad_lifetime_action_test.go#L81) | unit/verify | revert, verified |
| negative | [`TestRFC4301SADLifetimeTerminatesTheSAAtTheEndOfItsInterval`](https://github.com/ze-software/ze/blob/main/internal/component/ike/engine/rfc4301_sad_lifetime_test.go#L52) | unit/verify | revert, verified |

### [`RFC4301-4.4.2.1-3`](#rfc4301-4.4.2.1-3)

Note that implementations MUST be able to handle having the counters at the ends of an SA get out of synch, e.g., because of packet loss or because the implementations at each end of the SA aren't doing things the same way. (§4.4.2.1)

Audit verdict: unimplemented (no code path enforces the requirement), stale-unit: internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmStateFromParams moved. Rejudged the current xfrmStateFromParams and newLifetimeState against RFC 4301 Section 4.4.2.1: Note that implementations MUST be able to handle having the counters at the ends of an SA get out of synch, e.g., because of packet loss or because the implementations at each end of the SA aren't doing things the same way. The current state builder projects selector scope through xfrmStateScopeFromParams and still sets no byte lifetime in state.Limits. newLifetimeState initializes softTime and hardTime from a time lifetime; lifetimeState.softBytes and byteCount have no non-test producer writes. No byte-count lifetime exists whose end counters could be handled. The explicit gap remains accurate and has no test tags; this rejudgment refreshes the cited producer without upgrading the absent capability.

No test carries RFC4301-4.4.2.1-3, so there is no linked test.

### [`RFC4301-5.1-4`](#rfc4301-5.1-4)

Match the packet headers against the cache for the SPD specified by the SPD-ID from step 1. (§5.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), shifted: internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_boundary_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301BoundarySPDEntryCarriesSelectorDirectionOrderAndTemplate, internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_boundary_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301BoundarySPDEntryIsRefusedRatherThanWidened moved. Re-judged 2026-11-10 (independent judge, authored none of spec-rfc4301-architecture-gaps). The stale unit TestRFC4301BoundarySPDEntryIsRefusedRatherThanWidened now refuses a port with a bit outside its mask (443 and 0x0100, each under 0x00ff) in both directions, where it refused a 0x00ff mask outright: xfrmSelectorPort now holds a mask-aligned partial mask exactly (it is how an ICMP code block is installed) and refuses only port bits the kernel would ignore. The RFC4301-5.1-4 negative claim says exactly that refusal for the outbound entry. Every earlier refusal case stays, a second input is added, so the unit proves at least what it did. The stack proof stays the two engine outbound-steps units, unchanged. Verdict stands. Prior judgement: Step 2 (match the outbound packet headers against the SPD). Linux keeps no SPD cache, so the match is the kernel SPD lookup over the entries Ze installs through createFirstChildSA, installSPDPolicies and installUnmatched. Positive: from one source, a datagram to 10.1.0.5 meets the Child SA PROTECT entry (ESP at the tunnel endpoint under child.OutboundSPI, XfrmOutNoStates unchanged) and one to 10.3.0.5:7001 meets the operator BYPASS entry (delivered clear, no ESP). Negative: 10.3.0.5 on port 7009, differing from the BYPASS entry only by port, is not bypassed: EPERM, XfrmOutPolBlock +1, not delivered, so the match reads the headers, not the destination alone. Judge ran both units in their own user+net namespace: PASS, not skipped. Records: xfrmPolicyFromParams panic revert red both polarities; author's manual overlays (bypass->BLOCK reddens the positive, discard->ALLOW reddens the negative, scratch p7-ov-*.log) confirm each polarity reads the disposition. Re-judged 2026-09-30 (leftovers): the retired lead-in RFC4301-5.1-1's tags moved here onto the dataplane SPD boundary units, as supplementary builder-level cover of step 2's input: the positive asserts the outbound entry's out direction, 10.0.0.0/24 -> 10.1.0.0/24 prefixes and exact source port 443; the negative asserts a 0x00ff port mask is refused (nil policy) for SADirOut and SADirIn rather than widened. Claims match the assertions. Records: xfrmPolicyFromParams panic revert observed red both polarities. Judge ran both: PASS. Stack proof stays the two engine units.

### [`RFC4301-5.1-5`](#rfc4301-5.1-5)

If there is a match, then process the packet as specified by the matching cache entry, i.e., BYPASS, DISCARD, or PROTECT using AH or ESP. (§5.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Step 3a: process as the matching entry says, BYPASS, DISCARD or PROTECT. PROTECT: the datagram reaches the remote endpoint as ESP carrying the Child SA outbound SPI, no XfrmOutNoStates. BYPASS: delivered in the clear, and no ESP copy on the raw socket. DISCARD: operator DISCARD entry refuses the write with EPERM, XfrmOutPolBlock +1, not delivered. All three dispositions asserted on kernel counters and delivery; AH is not installed by IKE (ESP only), so the PROTECT case is ESP. Records: producer revert red both polarities; overlays discard->ALLOW and bypass->BLOCK each redden exactly the unit that asserts that disposition.

### [`RFC4301-5.1-6`](#rfc4301-5.1-6)

If no match is found in the cache, search the SPD (SPD-S and SPD-O parts) specified by SPD-ID. (§5.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Step 3b SPD search. With no kernel SPD cache every packet, including the first of a flow, is looked up in the SPD (SPD-S: the Child SA PROTECT entry; SPD-O: operator BYPASS/DISCARD and the catch-all). Positive: first packets on the PROTECT and BYPASS entries take those entries' dispositions. Negative: a packet selecting no operator entry takes the catch-all's discard (EPERM, XfrmOutPolBlock +1), not a neighbouring entry's bypass. Claims name the cache absence rather than proving a cache. Records as 5.1-4.

### [`RFC4301-5.1-7`](#rfc4301-5.1-7)

If the SPD entry calls for PROTECT, i.e., creation of an SA, the key management mechanism (e.g., IKEv2) is invoked to create the SA. If SA creation succeeds, a new outbound (SPD-S) cache entry is created, along with outbound and inbound SAD entries, otherwise the packet is discarded. (§5.1)

Audit verdict: unimplemented (no code path enforces the requirement), fresh. Rejudged against RFC 4301 Section 5.1: If the SPD entry calls for PROTECT, i.e., creation of an SA, the key management mechanism (e.g., IKEv2) is invoked to create the SA. If SA creation succeeds, a new outbound (SPD-S) cache entry is created, along with outbound and inbound SAD entries, otherwise the packet is discarded. installChildSA now checks negotiated pairs before installation and installs their states and policies only after an IKE exchange already produced the Child SA. parseSPDPolicy accepts only bypass and discard operator entries; it does not register packet-triggered negotiation. The current non-vendor internal, pkg and cmd trees contain no XFRM_MSG_ACQUIRE or XFRMNLGRP_ACQUIRE consumer. The state-scope and selector changes do not add an acquire path. The declared packet-triggered key-management gap therefore remains unimplemented.

No test carries RFC4301-5.1-7, so there is no linked test.

### [`RFC4301-5.1.2.1-1`](#rfc4301-5.1.2.1-1)

If the packet will immediately enter a domain for which the DSCP value in the outer header is not appropriate, that value MUST be mapped to an appropriate value for the domain (§5.1.2.1)

Records cover only part of this requirement: tested "that value MUST be mapped"; gap "to an appropriate value for the domain"; dscp-map copies the inner DSCP or fixes one outer DSCP per peer, and the tags prove that treatment reaches the SA on VPP for any value and on Linux XFRM for copy and 0, and that a value a dataplane cannot write is refused at commit and again at install rather than installed as another; Gap is an appropriate value Ze cannot write: a fixed value 1 to 63 on Linux XFRM, which internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmDSCPFlag refuses because the XFRM state writes only the inner DSCP or 0 (LL-XFRM-OUTER-DF-SET-DSCP, docs/architecture/linux-limitations.md, a design constraint and not a kernel limit: an nftables rewrite of the outer header could write it), an owner-decided refusal never counted conformant whose Ze kernel patch is planned after the first release (plan/post-release/kernel/spec-xfrm-outer-df-set-dscp.md), and a per-class mapping table, which is not built; plan/release/fixes/spec-rfc4301-architecture-gaps.md; they do not verify the whole requirement.

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Step 3a SAD lookup of an ESP packet addressed to Ze. Positive: ESP on the Child SA inbound SPI, sealed with the kernel key around an inner packet inside the selectors, is delivered with XfrmInNoStates and XfrmInStateProtoError unchanged. ### [`RFC4301-5.2-8`](#rfc4301-5.2-8)

If the packet is not addressed to the device or is addressed to this device and is not AH or ESP, look up the packet header in the (appropriate) SPD-I cache. If there is a match and the packet is to be discarded or bypassed, do so. (§5.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Step 3b SPD-I lookup of a clear packet addressed to Ze. Positive: UDP from 10.3.0.5 to local port 7101 matches the operator in-BYPASS entry ranked ahead of a DISCARD catch-all: delivered, no XfrmInPolBlock. Negative: to port 7102 on the operator in-DISCARD entry with a BYPASS catch-all: XfrmInPolBlock +1, not delivered, so only the matched entry can have dropped it. Each unit isolates its disposition by the opposite catch-all. Judge ran both: PASS. Records: xfrmPolicyFromParams revert red both; overlays bypass->BLOCK / discard->ALLOW redden exactly the matching polarity (scratch p7-ovin-*.log). Not-addressed-to-device (forwarded) packets are not probed; the SPD-I lookup is the same fwd/in policy check.

### [`RFC4301-5.2-9`](#rfc4301-5.2-9)

(No SAs are created in response to receipt of a packet that requires IPsec protection; only BYPASS or DISCARD cache entries can be created this way.) If there is no match, discard the traffic. (§5.2)

Audit verdict: unimplemented (no code path enforces the requirement), fresh. Rejudged against RFC 4301 Section 5.2 step 3b: (No SAs are created in response to receipt of a packet that requires IPsec protection; only BYPASS or DISCARD cache entries can be created this way.) If there is no match, discard the traffic. The current IKE XFRM lifecycle installs final disposition first and removes it last: installUnmatched applies the owned namespace FWD default and explicit IN/OUT catch-alls; interface FWD catch-alls are omitted only under that shared default. applyConfig installs this state before operator entries and peers. shutdown stops peers before releaseSPD, which preserves final disposition when warnSPDLeft reports leftovers; removal releases the FWD default only after explicit removals succeed. The known missing cases are unchanged: VPP runs no node-wide final entry, and the independent OSPF manual-key installer can hold protocol-89 SPD entries without the IKE catch-all. No code in these changes supplies final discard to those databases. The explicit gap remains unimplemented.

No test carries RFC4301-5.2-9, so there is no linked test.

### [`RFC4301-5.2-10`](#rfc4301-5.2-10)

Apply AH or ESP processing as specified, using the SAD entry selected in step 3a above. (§5.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Step 4 first sentence: ESP processing with the SAD entry step 3a selected. Positive: the packet on the Child SA SPI is decrypted with that SA's key and delivered, XfrmInStateProtoError unchanged. Negative: right SPI, fresh sequence number, sealed with a zero key: ICV fails, XfrmInStateProtoError +1, not delivered, so processing uses the selected SA's key and not a pass-through. Judge ran both: PASS. Records: xfrmStateFromParams revert red both. AH processing (OSPFv3) not probed here.

### [`RFC4301-6.2-3`](#rfc4301-6.2-3)

If no SA exists that would carry the outbound ICMP message in question, and if no SPD entry would allow carriage of this outbound ICMP error message, then an IPsec implementation MUST map the message to the SA that would carry the return traffic associated with the packet that triggered the ICMP error message. (§6.2)

Records cover only part of this requirement: tested "if no SPD entry would allow carriage of this outbound ICMP error message"; gap "MUST map the message to the SA that would carry the return traffic associated with the packet that triggered the ICMP error message"; the kernel maps an error into the return SA also when a BYPASS entry would carry it in clear, which this condition excludes, so internal/component/ike/engine/icmp_overlap.go::refuseICMPErrorBypass refuses at commit, by owner decision, `unmatched bypass` and every out or both bypass entry carrying ICMP error types towards the remote selectors of a peer whose icmp-error-check is true and which is not bound to a VTI (a VTI peer's policies carry its if_id, which the kernel's reverse lookup at if_id 0 never finds), and the tags prove that refusal and its accepted controls; Gap is the mapping itself: no namespace test observes an error Ze generates being sent into the return SA (XFRM_POLICY_ICMP), and a forwarded error under `unmatched discard` is blocked by the out catch-all before the kernel maps it, a Ze-owed design on the stock kernel (LL-XFRM-ICMP-TRANSIT, docs/architecture/linux-limitations.md, a design constraint); plan/release/fixes/spec-rfc4301-architecture-gaps.md; they do not verify the whole requirement.

Audit verdict: not reviewed: no reviewer has checked whether these tests enforce the requirement

### [`RFC4301-6.2-4`](#rfc4301-6.2-4)

If an IPsec implementation receives an inbound ICMP error message on an SA, and the IP and ICMP headers of the message do not match the traffic selectors for the SA, the receiver MUST process the received message in a special fashion. Specifically, the receiver must extract the header of the triggering packet from the ICMP payload, and reverse fields as described above to determine if the packet is consistent with the selectors for the SA via which the ICMP error message was received. (§6.2)

Records cover only part of this requirement: tested "the receiver MUST process the received message in a special fashion"; gap "If an IPsec implementation receives an inbound ICMP error message on an SA"; the split is by case, not by clause: Tested covers an ICMP error addressed to Ze, which the kernel delivers when its embedded header, reversed, matches the SA's inbound policy and drops when it does not, on the state internal/component/ike/dataplane/xfrm_linux.go::xfrmStateFromParams installs with XFRM_STATE_ICMP (TestICMPErrorPayloadHeaderCheckedOnLocalDelivery); Gap is a forwarded ICMP error that arrives on an SA, which is dropped unread because Ze's fwd catch-all is a policy and so keeps xfrm_in_fwd_icmp from running; Ze owes the fix, the node-wide fwd catch-all moved to the netns XFRM default policy and the fwd interface catch-all removed (LL-XFRM-ICMP-TRANSIT, docs/architecture/linux-limitations.md, a design constraint and not a kernel limit); plan/release/fixes/spec-rfc4301-architecture-gaps.md; they do not verify the whole requirement.

Audit verdict: not reviewed: no reviewer has checked whether these tests enforce the requirement

### [`RFC4301-7.1-2`](#rfc4301-7.1-2)

If the SA will carry traffic for specified protocols, the selector set for the SA MUST specify the port fields (or ICMP type/code or Mobility Header type) as ANY. (§7.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-19 (independent judge, after 4c41f84038, which replaced childPolicyParams with childPolicies: one inbound and one outbound policy per negotiated pair, all bound to the one SA, plus inboundStateSelector for the transport-mode state). RFC 4301 Section 7.1: "If the SA will carry traffic for specified protocols, the selector set for the SA MUST specify the port fields (or ICMP type/code or Mobility Header type) as ANY." Prior verdict enforced. selectorPort now takes the side (tsSelector) instead of (child, local); childPolicies picks local/remote by SelectorsLocalIsTSi exactly as the old side choice did, and the ANY/single/OPAQUE mapping is byte-identical. The units changed only to childPolicies(rfc4301ChildWith(pair), dir)[0]; rfc4301ChildWith carries one pair, so the slice holds one policy. Each negotiated pair now gets its own protocol and ports, so a multi-pair SA no longer inherits pair 0's. Positive asserts UpperProto 17 and both ports ANY in both directions; negative asserts no ExactPortMatch(8080/9090/0) leaks under ANY. PASS at HEAD. Verdict stands. Owed: both revert records (break on selectorPort) report unit-changed and must be re-observed.

### [`RFC4301-7.1-3`](#rfc4301-7.1-3)

If the SA will carry traffic without regard to a specific protocol value (i.e., ANY is specified as the (Next Layer) protocol selector value), then the port field values are undefined and MUST be set to ANY as well. (§7.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-19 (independent judge, after 4c41f84038, which replaced childPolicyParams with childPolicies: one inbound and one outbound policy per negotiated pair, all bound to the one SA, plus inboundStateSelector for the transport-mode state). RFC 4301 Section 7.1: "If the SA will carry traffic without regard to a specific protocol value (i.e., ANY is specified as the (Next Layer) protocol selector value), then the port field values are undefined and MUST be set to ANY as well." Prior verdict enforced. TestRFC4301ProtocolAnyChildSACarriesAnyPorts changed only to childPolicies(child, dir)[0] over a one-pair narrowing result; it still asserts UpperProto 0 and both ports ANY in and out. The negative (programmableSelector refuses protocol ANY with a port) and the ipsec supplementary units are unchanged. The new transport-state cover also widens ports to ANY whenever the protocol widens to any (inboundStateSelector), consistent with this sentence. PASS at HEAD. Verdict stands. Owed: the positive's revert record (break on selectorPort) reports unit-changed and must be re-observed.

### [`RFC4301-7.3-4`](#rfc4301-7.3-4)

However, a receiver MUST discard non-initial fragments that arrive on an SA with non-trivial port (or ICMP type/code or MH type) selector values unless this feature has been negotiated. (§7.3)

Audit verdict: weak (the tests pass over code that does not enforce the requirement), shifted: internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_boundary_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301BoundarySPDEntryCarriesSelectorDirectionOrderAndTemplate, internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_boundary_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301BoundarySPDEntryIsRefusedRatherThanWidened moved. Rejudged Section 7.3: However, a receiver MUST discard non-initial fragments that arrive on an SA with non-trivial port (or ICMP type/code or MH type) selector values unless this feature has been negotiated. The tagged boundary positive asserts exact source port 443 on one inbound policy; its negative refuses a port outside its mask. TestNarrowingRefusesASelectorEveryFragmentMatches rejects TCP port 0 and ICMP type/code 0 while accepting unchanged nonzero and ANY controls. programmableSelector and childPolicyPair still enforce those checks before installing a policy. These are meaningful selector-shape assertions, but none of the tagged tests sends a non-initial fragment to a receiving SA and observes the required discard. In particular the boundary assertions cannot distinguish a receiver that accepts such fragments after reassembly or under a sibling's compatible policy. The current multi-pair tunnel state can remain unbound to its complete selector set. The old enforced note itself recorded that residual sibling-policy issue without withdrawing whole credit. Whole receive-side enforcement is not established by these tags, so weak replaces the overstated verdict without deleting their useful proof.

### [`RFC4301-7.3-5`](#rfc4301-7.3-5)

Also, the receiver MUST discard non-initial fragments that do not comply with the security policy applied to the overall packet. (§7.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), shifted: internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_boundary_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301BoundarySPDEntryCarriesSelectorDirectionOrderAndTemplate, internal/component/ike/dataplane/rfc4301_boundary_linux_test.go::TestRFC4301BoundarySPDEntryIsRefusedRatherThanWidened moved. Rejudged RFC 4301 Section 7.3: "Also, the receiver MUST discard non-initial fragments that do not comply with the security policy applied to the overall packet." All four existing covers were reread; their tags and claims are unchanged. The two packet covers install the original port-constrained tunnel Child selectors through the production creator, but explicitly opt into fixture-only noninitial-fragment permission before the real backend installs each state. The helper reads both actual installed states, requires permission true and the complete original directional selectors, and separately requires the production childStateScope permission to remain false. This supplies the fragment-admission precondition without claiming bilateral NON_FIRST_FRAGMENTS_ALSO negotiation, which production IKE does not perform. Both cases split the original inner UDP shape and send non-initial before initial, each in its own authenticated ESP packet. The conforming source must reassemble and deliver with XfrmInNoPols unchanged. The outside-source packet must not deliver and must produce exactly one XfrmInStateMismatch with XfrmInNoPols and XfrmInPolBlock unchanged; explicit permission prevents a missing-permission refusal from standing in for that selector check. The boundary positive supplement pins the inbound port selector and transform template; the boundary negative supplement requires malformed selectors to be refused rather than widened and a BYPASS policy to carry no template. The real kernel scope matcher checks the reassembled flow against the receiving state and records StateMismatch on refusal. Retain the existing enforced classification for this whole-packet policy-discard row, with no wider RFC conformance promotion. This is not evidence for the separate unnegotiated-fragment rule RFC4301-7.3-4, IKE negotiation, mixed-protection-context separation or a deliberately broken-kernel discriminator.

### [`RFC4301-8.2.1-1`](#rfc4301-8.2.1-1)

When such traffic arrives, if the traffic would exceed the updated PMTU value the traffic MUST be handled as follows: Case 1: Original (cleartext) packet is IPv4 and has the DF bit set. The implementation SHOULD discard the packet and send a PMTU ICMP message. Case 2: Original (cleartext) packet is IPv4 and has the DF bit clear. The implementation SHOULD fragment (before or after encryption per its configuration) and then forward the fragments. It SHOULD NOT send a PMTU ICMP message. Case 3: Original (cleartext) packet is IPv6. The implementation SHOULD discard the packet and send a PMTU ICMP message. (§8.2.1)

No behavior belongs to this sentence that those rows do not already state, so a row for it only restates them. ## Superseded

No document obsoletes RFC 4301, so its obligations are stated where they were written.
